Binding is never decided per agency API, and confidential clients, which authenticate with their registered key and keep bearer tokens, are unaffected (D82).

The branch carries the proof verifier, the binding of the authorization request and code, the bound code exchange, the `DPoP` scheme at the user-information and document-image endpoints, the per-client setting and its onboarding warnings, and the removal of the earlier per-API flag. The exchange, lifecycle, introspection and SAML branches above it only call the verifier and compare thumbprints, gated on the same predicate, so they stay pure delegation logic (D64).

## Functional requirements

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **FR-TOK-19** | All proofs of identity or possession presented to Login.gov by service providers and agencies, including key-possession proofs, MUST be short-lived and protected against replay. A key-possession proof is required of a public-client service provider only when its record sets `dpop_required` (FR-TOK-24); every proof that is presented is held to this row. (D82) |
| **FR-TOK-22** | Login.gov MUST support binding a delegated token to a key the service provider holds (DPoP, RFC 9449): when the exchange or renewal request carries a proof signed by that key, the token MUST be usable only with a fresh proof from the same key, and renewal of a bound token MUST require the same key. Whether binding applies is a per-client setting on the service provider's record (`dpop_required`, public clients only), never a per-API setting: an agency does not require or waive binding for its API. A public-client service provider approved for delegation whose record sets it MUST be bound end to end and MUST be refused without a proof wherever one is due; one whose record does not set it holds bearer tokens end to end, its exchange carrying `client_id` alone and its delegated tokens, renewal credentials and verification responses carrying no binding. Every browser-based service provider is recommended to set it before production. (D47, D82) |
| **FR-TOK-23** | Login.gov MUST report the binding to the agency when it verifies a token (the key thumbprint and a token type of `DPoP`), and the agency MUST verify a proof from that key on every request and refuse a bound token presented without one. A key-bound SAML assertion carries the thumbprint as an attribute. |
| **FR-TOK-24** | Binding MUST be mandatory for a public-client service provider whose record sets `dpop_required`, and MUST cover the service provider's own access token and renewal credential as well as the delegated tokens. Login.gov MUST verify the proof wherever it accepts one of those tokens, including its user-information endpoint, and MUST refuse a bound token presented without a proof. A public-client service provider whose record does not set it is a bearer client end to end, at the exchange included, and MUST NOT be asked for a proof or a key thumbprint. The setting MUST be ignored, with a warning at onboarding, on a confidential client; service providers that are confidential clients MUST be unaffected. Onboarding MUST warn when a delegation-approved public client does not set it and when a confidential client does. (D82) |
| **FR-TOK-25** | For a public-client service provider whose record sets `dpop_required`, the authorization code MUST be bound to the same key at the authorization request (RFC 9449 §10), so an intercepted code cannot be redeemed with another key; its authorization request without a key thumbprint MUST be refused. A public-client service provider whose record does not set it is not asked for one. (D82) |

Rows owned by other branches that this branch's call sites serve: FR-TOK-2 and the exchange proof (section 6), FR-TOK-12 and FR-TOK-14 for renewal and revocation (section 7), FR-VER-2 for the service provider's own verification (section 8), FR-TOK-9 for the thumbprint attribute on a SAML assertion (section 9), FR-OPS-1 for the proof algorithms in discovery (section 10).

## Protocol rows

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **EXC-17** | DPoP is mandatory for a public service provider (`pkce: true`) whose record sets `dpop_required` (`ServiceProvider#dpop_bound?`): the authorization-code grant, the token-exchange grant, the refresh grant, `/api/openid_connect/userinfo` and its own introspection each require a valid proof, and a token issued to it carries `token_type: DPoP` and `cnf.jkt`. A request from such a client without a proof fails with `invalid_dpop_proof` (RFC 9449 §5) or, at userinfo, `401` with `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP` (§7.1). The setting is ignored on a confidential client, and onboarding warns when it is set there. A public service provider approved for delegation whose record does not set it follows EXC-19a, and onboarding warns when it writes such a record ("its delegated tokens will be bearer tokens"). Confidential clients and their bearer tokens are unaffected. (D82) |
| **EXC-18** | `/api/openid_connect/userinfo` MUST verify the DPoP proof for a bound service-provider access token (`htm`, `htu`, `iat`, `jti`, `ath`, thumbprint equal to the token's `cnf.jkt`) and MUST refuse a bound token presented as a bearer token (RFC 9449 §7.1). Delegated tokens remain refused at userinfo regardless of proof (EXC-6). |
| **EXC-19** | A public service provider whose record sets `dpop_required` MUST send `dpop_jkt` on the authorization request (RFC 9449 §10); a request from it without `dpop_jkt` is refused with `dpop_jkt_required`. Login.gov binds the issued authorization code to that thumbprint and refuses a code exchange whose proof key differs. A public service provider whose record does not set it is not asked for `dpop_jkt`; a well-formed value is still accepted from any client and stored only when binding applies. (D82) |
| **EXC-19a** | A public service provider approved for delegation whose record does not set `dpop_required` is a bearer client end to end. Its code exchange issues a bearer access token (`token_type: Bearer`); the token-exchange grant accepts that bearer subject token with `client_id` alone and no proof; the delegated tokens it receives are bearer tokens with no `cnf`, introspection reports no binding, a SAML assertion issued to it carries no `dpop_jkt` attribute, and the refresh grant carries no proof. The other controls are unchanged: the 15-minute access-token lifetime, refresh rotation with reuse detection, the revocation cascades and the consent checks. The exposure key binding closes for a flagged client (a token taken from the browser replayed by its holder) is accepted for such a client as an onboarding trade-off, and every delegation-approved public service provider is expected to set `dpop_required` before production. (D82) |

Rows owned by other branches: EXC-2 and EXC-9 (client handling and the proof at the exchange, section 6), REF-2 (bound refresh tokens, section 7), INT-4 and INT-9 (`cnf.jkt` and `token_type` in the verification response, section 8), DISC-2 (`dpop_signing_alg_values_supported`, section 10), RS-DPOP-1 (the agency's obligation, section 15.3).

## As built

1. `DpopProofVerifier` (`app/services/dpop_proof_verifier.rb`) runs the RFC 9449 §4.3 checks in order: `typ` `dpop+jwt` (`PROOF_TYPE`), `alg` in `ALLOWED_ALGORITHMS` (`ES256`, `RS256`), a public-only `jwk`, the signature, `htm`, `htu` normalized (scheme and host lowercased, default port dropped, trailing slash ignored, query and fragment removed), `iat` within `dpop_proof_max_age_seconds` (300) either side of now, `ath` when a token accompanies the proof and absent otherwise (`Digest::SHA256.urlsafe_base64digest`), the expected thumbprint when the token is already bound, and the single-use `jti` last so a rejected proof never consumes one. Missing proof, key mismatch and replay have their own `error_description`; every other failure shares one, so the response does not help tune a forged proof. No server nonce is issued (D22).
2. `ReplayGuard.first_use?(namespace:, scope:, value:, ttl:)` (`app/services/replay_guard.rb`) is the one Redis `SET NX` for single-use values, keyed by namespace and a SHA-256 of presenter and value, so one caller cannot burn another's values; the proof `jti` is recorded under the key thumbprint for twice the window (E98). The token-exchange branch records client-assertion `jti` values through the same guard.
3. `service_providers.dpop_required` (boolean, default false), set through the configuration repository and through the Dashboard once it has the field. `ServiceProvider#dpop_bound?` is `pkce` and `dpop_required` and is the only predicate the forms, the linker and the verifier call sites read. `ServiceProvider#warn_if_key_binding_misconfigured`, run by `ServiceProviderSeeder` and `ServiceProviderUpdater` after every write, warns when a confidential client sets the flag (ignored) and when a public client approved for delegation lacks it ("its delegated tokens will be bearer tokens").
4. `OpenidConnectAuthorizeForm#dpop_jkt`: required when `dpop_binding_required?` (the record's `dpop_bound?`), refused as `dpop_jkt_required` when missing for such a client, accepted well-formed from any client (`BASE64URL_SHA256_FORMAT`, 43 base64url characters, shared with the S256 `code_challenge` check; `dpop_jkt_invalid` otherwise), and stored through `IdentityLinker#link_identity(dpop_jkt:)` on `identities.dpop_jkt` only when binding applies.
5. `OpenidConnectTokenForm#validate_dpop_proof` runs after every other check for a bound client: a code whose identity has no thumbprint is refused (`dpop_code_unbound`); the `DPoP` header is verified once per form, without `ath`, against `identities.dpop_jkt`; the response reports `token_type: DPoP` for a bound client and `Bearer` otherwise; every proof problem is `invalid_dpop_proof`. `OpenidConnect::TokenController#form_params` reads the proof from the `DPoP` request header only; a body field is not permitted.
6. `AccessTokenVerifier` reads the `Bearer` and `DPoP` schemes; `#verify_key_binding` refuses a bound token under `Bearer` (`bound_token_requires_dpop`) and an unbound token under `DPoP` (`token_not_bound`) with `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP algs="ES256 RS256", error="invalid_token"`, and a failing proof with `error="invalid_dpop_proof"`. `OpenidConnect::UserInfoController` and `OpenidConnect::DocumentImagesController` pass the header, method and URL and set the challenge. `#load_identity` reads `identities` only, so delegated tokens stay refused with or without a proof.
7. Removed: `token_exchange_resource_servers.dpop_required` (migration `20261009120000_remove_dpop_required_from_token_exchange_resource_servers`, under `safety_assured`), its factory trait, the seeder and updater expectations and the per-API fixture keys; binding is never decided per API (D47).
8. Migrations: `20261009100500_add_dpop_jkt_to_identities` (string, `sensitive=false`) and the removal above. Configuration: `dpop_proof_max_age_seconds` (300) in `lib/identity_config.rb` and `config/application.yml.default`. Locale keys in en, es, fr and zh: `openid_connect.authorization.errors.dpop_jkt_required`, `openid_connect.authorization.errors.dpop_jkt_invalid`, `openid_connect.token.errors.dpop_code_unbound`, `openid_connect.user_info.errors.bound_token_requires_dpop`, `openid_connect.user_info.errors.token_not_bound`, and the `invalid_dpop_proof` descriptions `openid_connect.token.errors.dpop_proof_required`, `dpop_proof_invalid` and `dpop_proof_replayed`.
9. Fixture: `config/delegated_access.localdev.yml` and the configuration repository's `delegated_access.yml` set `dpop_required: true` on the reference browser client (MyBenefits Assistant), so the end-to-end harness keeps exercising the bound path; `spec/features/openid_connect/delegated_access_consent_spec.rb` sends `dpop_jkt` as that client does.
10. Specs: `spec/services/dpop_proof_verifier_spec.rb`, `spec/services/replay_guard_spec.rb`, `spec/services/access_token_verifier_spec.rb`, `spec/services/identity_linker_spec.rb`, `spec/forms/openid_connect_authorize_form_spec.rb`, `spec/forms/openid_connect_token_form_spec.rb`, `spec/requests/openid_connect/token_spec.rb`, `spec/requests/openid_connect_userinfo_spec.rb` (a confidential client's bearer request compared byte for byte with and without a stray `DPoP` header), the consent feature spec, `spec/factories/token_exchange_resource_servers.rb`, and `spec/support/delegated_access_helper.rb` (`DelegatedAccessHelper` for client assertions, `DpopHelper` with one P-256 key per example), both included from `spec/rails_helper.rb`. Verified locally: 361 examples across the branch's specs and 54 across the specs the per-API removal touches; rubocop clean.
11. Stays elsewhere by design: `ResourceServerAuthenticator` and `DelegatedAccessClientHandling` (sections 6 and 7), the CORS rules for the revocation and verification endpoints (sections 7 and 8), the discovery members `dpop_signing_alg_values_supported` and `none` (section 10).
12. Key binding follows the per-client flag (D82, built 2026-10-13). `service_providers.dpop_required` (boolean, default false; migration `20261013100000_add_dpop_required_to_service_providers`) and `ServiceProvider#dpop_bound?` (`pkce` and `dpop_required`) replace the former "public client approved for delegation" predicate in `OpenidConnectAuthorizeForm#dpop_binding_required?` and `OpenidConnectTokenForm#dpop_binding_required?`. A flagged public client must send `dpop_jkt` (`dpop_jkt_required` in en, es, fr and zh) and proves possession at the code exchange, the token exchange, refresh, revocation, userinfo and its own introspection exactly as before. An unflagged public client is a bearer client: its `dpop_jkt` is accepted well-formed and ignored, its sign-in token is bearer, the exchange identifies it by `client_id` alone and issues bearer delegated tokens (`token_type: Bearer`, no `cnf` on introspection, no `dpop_jkt` SAML attribute), and refresh, revocation and its own introspection need no proof. Binding then follows the token, not the record: a bound token or family presented by any client still demands its key (exchange and refresh answer `invalid_dpop_proof`, introspection answers a DPoP challenge, revocation leaves the token untouched and answers `200 {}`), and a flagged client presenting an unbound token is refused as before. `ServiceProvider#warn_if_key_binding_misconfigured` is called by `ServiceProviderSeeder` and `ServiceProviderUpdater`: it warns when a delegation-approved public client lacks the flag ("its delegated tokens will be bearer tokens") and when the flag is set on a confidential record (ignored). The local fixture (both environment sections) and the configuration repository's `delegated_access.yml` set `dpop_required: true` on the reference browser client. Commits: dpop `d18cf57b12`, token-exchange `cfa3044697`, token-lifecycle `c24507a5eb`, introspection `e916a6bbb2`, with fixups on saml-assertions, operations and config-content; verified with 2,150 examples of the delegated-access set at the top of the stack, rubocop, the analytics and tracker lints and gitleaks clean.

## Decisions

- **D19** the service provider is a browser-based public client with DPoP; confidential clients unchanged (full entry in section 16).
- **D20** binding is a property of the service provider record, not a per-request option; narrowed by D82 to the record's `dpop_required`.
- **D21** the authorization code is bound to the key through `dpop_jkt`.
- **D22** server nonces (RFC 9449 §8) stay deferred; freshness rests on the `iat` window and single-use `jti`.
- **D46** proof algorithms are ES256 and RS256.
- **D47** no per-API `dpop_required` flag; binding is never decided per agency API.
- **D64** the key-binding code lives in this one branch, before the exchange.
- **D82** key binding is a per-client setting, `service_providers.dpop_required`, public clients only, strict when set, bearer end to end when not, independent of delegation approval.

## How to review

- Diff against `delegated-access-site-keys`.
- Read `DpopProofVerifier` once against RFC 9449 §4.3 and check the order of checks, with the `jti` last; then `ServiceProvider#dpop_bound?` and confirm it is the only predicate (never `pkce` plus delegation approval) behind `dpop_binding_required?` on both forms, the `identities.dpop_jkt` write and `verify_key_binding`.
- Check that a flagged client is refused without `dpop_jkt` at authorize and without a proof at the code exchange and userinfo; that an unflagged public client approved for delegation receives `token_type: Bearer` at the code exchange and never sees a challenge; that the flag has no effect on a confidential client beyond the warning; and that both seeder and updater warnings fire.
- Specs that prove it: the verifier spec, the replay guard spec, the token request spec, the userinfo request spec, the authorize and token form specs, the service provider model, seeder and updater specs.
- Must not change for existing clients: confidential clients and public clients without the flag keep bearer tokens and never see a challenge; `dpop_jkt` is optional for them; the authorization-code grant's responses are otherwise unchanged.
- Known open items: the Dashboard needs the `dpop_required` field before partners can set it there; every delegation-approved public client should set the flag before production; `Addressable` for `htu` normalization is held until the harness run; D22 is revisited if partner clock skew, pre-computed proofs or an agency's request for nonces appears.

# 6. `delegated-access-token-exchange`: token exchange at the token endpoint

**Stack position** 6 of 14, on `delegated-access-dpop`. **Historical plan section** 5.4.

## Objective

A service provider that holds a person's approval obtains a delegated token for exactly one agency API by presenting the access token of the person's sign-in at Login.gov's existing token endpoint, following OAuth 2.0 Token Exchange (RFC 8693). The exchange is a delegation, not an impersonation: the agency later learns, through verification, that the person is the subject and the service provider is the party acting. No separate service or route is introduced; the grant type selects the exchange form at the endpoint every integration already uses.

The design keeps every control that direct sign-in has and adds none that is weaker. The caller proves who it is before anything about the registry or the person's approvals is revealed: a confidential client with a signed client assertion over its registered key, a public client with its client identifier and, when its record requires key binding, a proof of possession of the key its sign-in token is bound to. The subject token must belong to that caller and its sign-in must still be live, so an old or copied token cannot start a new delegation later. The request names exactly one registered API, the person must hold a live, current approval of the application that owns it, and the issued token is an opaque random reference whose meaning exists only at Login.gov: it is valid for one API, for fifteen minutes or less, cannot be exchanged again, and cannot read the person's attributes at the user-information endpoint. Nothing is written for the agency or the application, so a delegation never creates or revives a connection the person did not make.

The token's format is a fact about the agency's API, so the API's registration decides whether an OAuth token or a SAML assertion is issued; the service provider may name a format but does not need to (D70). A refusal for a missing or lapsed approval names the application's delegation scope, so the service provider can send the person through a sign-in that requests it; every other problem with the API is refused without detail. No ceiling refuses a registered service provider for volume: issuance is logged and monitored against alert thresholds, and runaway-caller protection belongs to the infrastructure layer.

## Functional requirements

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **FR-TOK-1** | Token issuance MUST follow the OAuth 2.0 Token Exchange standard (RFC 8693) at Login.gov's existing token endpoint; no separate service is introduced. A grant type the endpoint does not serve is refused with the standard `unsupported_grant_type` code. (D71) |
| **FR-TOK-2** | A service provider that is a confidential client MUST prove its identity at issuance with a cryptographic key it alone holds. A service provider that is a public client (one whose tokens are held in the person's browser) sends its client identifier instead and, when its record requires key binding (FR-TOK-24), MUST bind every token it receives to a key it holds (FR-TOK-22) and present a proof from that key at issuance, so a token stolen from the browser cannot be exchanged. Which of the two a service provider is MUST be fixed at onboarding. (D82) |
| **FR-TOK-3** | Each delegated token MUST be valid for exactly one agency API, named by the service provider in the request, so a token stolen from one agency API is useless at any other. |
| **FR-TOK-4** | Delegated tokens MUST be short-lived: 15 minutes by default. An agency MAY set a shorter maximum for one of its APIs (for example an API that makes changes); no API may have a longer one. |
| **FR-TOK-5** | A delegated token MUST identify the person to the agency, identify the service provider as the party acting, state the approved access, and carry a delegation identifier the agency can use to join the token to fraud-signal events and billing. |
| **FR-TOK-6** | A delegated token MUST NOT be usable to obtain further tokens, and MUST NOT be usable at Login.gov's user-information endpoint, with or without a key-possession proof. The person's identity attributes for an agency are available only through the agency's verification (section 8); the service provider never receives them through delegation. A time-boxed opening for an agency that cannot yet verify by introspection is documented in implementation plan 5.18 and is not built; the recommendation is against it, because that endpoint authenticates no caller. (D83) |
| **FR-TOK-7** | Issuance MUST be possible only while the person's Login.gov sign-in to the service provider is still active. Once a token has been issued, continued access rests on renewal (FR-TOK-10), not on the sign-in. |
| **FR-TOK-8** | Issuing a delegated token MUST NOT revive a connection the person revoked or alter a direct session the person has with the agency. |
| **FR-TOK-17** | A service provider MUST NOT be able to widen its own access beyond what Login.gov defined and the person approved. With one scope per application there is nothing to narrow either: a `scope` parameter on the issuance request is ignored and the issued scope is the application's. (D72) |
| **FR-TOK-20** | Login.gov MUST NOT impose a hard per-service-provider ceiling on issuance or renewal that refuses requests from a registered service provider: a service provider in nationwide use acts for many people at several agencies in quick succession. Login.gov MUST instead monitor issuance and renewal volumes and error rates per service provider against configurable alert thresholds; protection against a runaway caller belongs to the infrastructure layer. (D37) |
| **FR-TOK-21** | Delegated tokens MUST be opaque references, not signed tokens carrying claims; their meaning is established only by Login.gov (section 8). |
| **FR-TOK-26** | When issuance is refused because the person has not approved the application that owns the requested API, or that approval has lapsed, Login.gov MUST tell the service provider so and name the application's delegation scope, so the service provider can start a new sign-in requesting it. This MUST be told only to a service provider that has proved its identity, and only for a registered API; any other problem with the API is refused without that detail. (D31) |
| **FR-CEN-8** | Login.gov MUST issue a delegated token only for an API of an application the person approved, where the approval is still valid: not revoked, not expired, and not superseded by a material change in consent content. |
| **FR-CEN-16** | Disabling an agency, an agency API, or a service provider at Login.gov MUST stop all related delegated access at the next check. |

Rows owned by other branches that this branch enforces at the exchange: FR-TOK-19 and FR-TOK-22 (the proof and the replay protection, section 5), FR-CEN-10 and FR-CEN-12 (no effect at an unapproved application, no relationship created at the agency, section 2), FR-CEN-13 (revocation ends live tokens, section 7), FR-TOK-9 (the SAML format, section 9).

## Protocol rows

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **EXC-1** | Login.gov MUST implement RFC 8693 token exchange at the existing token endpoint, `POST /api/openid_connect/token`, selected by `grant_type=urn:ietf:params:oauth:grant-type:token-exchange`, with `subject_token_type=urn:ietf:params:oauth:token-type:access_token` and the RFC 8707 `resource` parameter naming exactly one registered resource server; a request naming two or more fails with `invalid_request`, since Login.gov issues one token with one audience. No new route is added for the exchange. (D30) |
| **EXC-2** | Client handling at the token endpoint depends on the client type fixed at onboarding. A confidential service provider authenticates the token-exchange and refresh grants with `private_key_jwt` (RFC 7523) against the certificates on its record, with `aud` the token endpoint URL; a request without a valid client assertion fails with `invalid_client`. A public service provider (`pkce: true`) sends `client_id` without a client assertion; when its record sets `dpop_required` it MUST present a DPoP proof (RFC 9449 §4.3) whose key thumbprint equals the binding of the subject or refresh token it presents, with `ath` over that token, and when it does not, it presents its bearer subject token with `client_id` alone (EXC-19a). `code_verifier` is refused on these grants. The token endpoint's existing CORS rule is unchanged. (D82) |
| **EXC-3** | Login.gov MUST mint only when all of the following hold: the subject token belongs to the authenticated service provider; the service provider is approved for delegation; the person is identity-verified with an active profile and the sign-in carries IAL2 (or IALmax with a verified user); a live, current approval (CON-11, CON-12) exists for the application that owns the requested `resource`; the resource server, its application, that application's agency and the service provider are all active. Otherwise it returns an RFC 8693 §2.2.2 error: `invalid_client` for authentication, `invalid_grant` for subject-token and session problems, `invalid_target` with one wording for a resource that is unknown, inactive or whose application excludes the caller, and `consent_required` with `error_description` naming the application's `token_exchange:<value>` scope when an authenticated caller names a registered, active resource that accepts it and the person has no live, current approval. (D31) |
| **EXC-4** | The minted token is an opaque random reference. The live token is a Redis entry keyed by its SHA-256 digest with a TTL equal to its lifetime, holding `aud` (the resource server identifier), `scope` (the application's approved delegation scope), the service provider as actor, the grant, the `delegation_id`, the forwarded IAL and AAL and its expiry; the `token_exchange_tokens` row is the secret-free issuance record (no digest, nothing read from it to decide validity), kept for billing, fraud-signal events, the account page and audit. Lifetime is `token_exchange_access_token_ttl_seconds` (default 900) or the resource server's `max_access_token_seconds` when that is lower; never higher. (D26, D27) |
| **EXC-5** | The exchange MUST NOT create, update or read the agency application's `identities` row, MUST NOT return an `id_token`, and MUST NOT create an authorization code. |
| **EXC-6** | A token minted by exchange MUST be rejected as `subject_token` (no chained delegation), and MUST be rejected by `/api/openid_connect/userinfo`. |
| **EXC-7** | The response MUST include `access_token`, `issued_token_type`, `token_type`, `expires_in`, `scope`, and, per §7, `refresh_token`. |
| **EXC-8** | The exchange is not rate-limited per service provider and never refuses a request for volume. Login.gov logs every exchange and failure through analytics and monitors volume and error rate per service provider against configurable alert thresholds; runaway-caller protection is the infrastructure layer's (edge and load balancer), not the application's. (D37) |
| **EXC-9** | Login.gov MUST accept an RFC 9449 `DPoP` header on the token-exchange grant. When present and valid (RFC 9449 §4.3: `typ`, `alg` `ES256` or `RS256`, public-only `jwk`, signature, `htm`, `htu`, `iat` within the acceptance window, unused `jti`, `ath` over the subject token), the minted token and its refresh family MUST be bound to the proof key by storing its RFC 7638 thumbprint (`dpop_jkt`), and `token_type` MUST be `DPoP` (`N_A` for a SAML assertion, which carries the thumbprint as a `dpop_jkt` attribute instead). A proof that is present but invalid MUST fail with `invalid_dpop_proof`. There is no per-resource-server requirement: whether a proof is due follows the service provider's record (EXC-17, EXC-19a), and a confidential service provider's exchange never carries one. The proof is checked after every other validation so an unauthenticated or unauthorized caller learns nothing. The proof's single-use `jti` and the client assertion's `jti` are recorded through one replay guard, each under its own namespace and the presenter's identity. (D46, D47, D82) |
| **EXC-10** | The implementation MUST follow the token endpoint's existing conventions: `OpenidConnect::TokenController#create` dispatches on `grant_type` to a form object; the form exposes `#submit` (returning a `FormResponse`) and `#response`; the controller logs the token event with the form's attributes and `analytics.sp_integration_errors_present` when `integration_errors` are present; parameters are read through `params.permit`; the response is rendered with `render json:` and status `:ok` on success or `:bad_request` on failure. |
| **EXC-11** | Error responses MUST keep the token endpoint's current shape, a JSON object with an `error` key and HTTP 400, and MUST set `error` to the RFC 6749 §5.2 / RFC 8693 §2.2.2 code (`invalid_client`, `invalid_grant`, `invalid_target`, `invalid_scope`, `unsupported_grant_type`, `invalid_request`) with the human-readable message in `error_description`. A `grant_type` that is not `authorization_code`, `refresh_token` or the RFC 8693 URN answers `unsupported_grant_type` through `OpenidConnectUnsupportedGrantForm`; an error path only, so existing authorization-code clients are unaffected. (D71) |
| **EXC-12** | The subject token's sign-in session MUST still be live at the first exchange (the same `OutOfBandSessionAccessor` TTL check `userinfo` applies); otherwise `invalid_grant`. Once minted, the delegated family no longer depends on that session (REF-6). (D28) |
| **EXC-13** | There is one scope per application (ONB-2), so there is nothing to narrow: a `scope` parameter on the exchange is ignored and the issued `scope` is the application's value. (D72) |
| **EXC-14** | The person's live approval of the application that owns the requested resource is exchanged into one token; that approval's `delegation_id` identifies the exchange to the agency (introspection, fraud-signal events, billing `request_id`), so the agency's join key is stable across renewals. |
| **EXC-15** | A caller that authenticates but is not approved for delegation fails with `invalid_client`, not `invalid_grant`: approval is a property of the client. |
| **EXC-16** | The exchange response MUST include `refresh_token_expires_in` (seconds until the family ends) alongside `expires_in`, and both MUST be computed from the single instant at which the issuance record was created, so a service provider can schedule refreshes without clock arithmetic against the response time. |
| **EXC-20** | Delegated tokens MUST NOT be JWTs or any other self-describing format; a resource server learns a token's meaning only by introspection (section 8), and the service provider learns nothing from the string. (D29) |
| **EXC-21** | `requested_token_type` is OPTIONAL (RFC 8693 §2.1). The issued format is the `token_format` registered for the resource server named in `resource`: a request that sends nothing, or sends the registered format's URN, is served; a request that sends the other URN is served in the registered format, which `issued_token_type` states and the exchange analytics log; a value that is neither URN fails with `invalid_request`. (D70) |

Rows owned by other branches that the exchange touches: EXC-17, EXC-18, EXC-19 and EXC-19a (section 5), REF-1 to REF-9 (the refresh family the exchange opens, section 7), INT-1 to INT-11 (how the live entry is read, section 8), SAML-1 to SAML-15 (the SAML format, section 9).

## As built

1. Dispatch: `OpenidConnect::TokenController#build_form` is a `case params[:grant_type]`: `authorization_code` goes to `OpenidConnectTokenForm`, `OpenidConnectTokenExchangeForm::GRANT_TYPE` (the RFC 8693 URN) to `OpenidConnectTokenExchangeForm` while `token_exchange_enabled` is on, and anything else (the URN with the flag off included) to `OpenidConnectUnsupportedGrantForm`, which answers `unsupported_grant_type` and carries the token event's attributes so the controller needs no special case. The exchange is logged as `openid_connect_token_exchange`, everything else as `openid_connect_token`. `#form_params` reads the RFC 9449 proof from the `DPoP` request header only.
2. Client handling by type (`OpenidConnectTokenExchangeForm#validate_client`, plumbing in `DelegatedAccessClientHandling`): a confidential service provider sends `client_assertion` and `client_assertion_type`, checked by `ResourceServerAuthenticator.new(client_assertion:, audience:, key_source: :service_provider)`: RFC 7523 with RS256 pinned, `iss` equal to `sub`, every certificate on the record tried so rotation does not break callers, `aud` naming the endpoint, `exp` at most five minutes after `iat` with ten seconds of leeway, the signature verified before the replay cache is touched, and the `jti` recorded through `ReplayGuard.first_use?` under the client identifier for the assertion lifetime plus leeway. The same class takes `key_source: :resource_server` for agencies at verification and revocation. A public service provider sends `client_id` naming a `pkce` record. The credentials are not interchangeable: a client assertion from a `pkce` record, or a bare `client_id` from a confidential record, is `invalid_client` (`client_type_mismatch`); `code_verifier` on this grant is `invalid_request`; a record that is not `delegation_service_provider?` is `invalid_client` (`client_not_approved`) before anything else is looked at.
3. Validation order, each check stopping at the first failure so nothing about the registry or the person's approvals reaches a caller that has not authenticated: the client (`invalid_client`); the request shape (`validate_request_shape`, `invalid_request`: `subject_token_type` the access-token URN, `subject_token` present and free of null bytes, `requested_token_type` absent or one of `REQUESTED_TOKEN_TYPES`, else `invalid_requested_token_type`, exactly one `resource`); the subject token (`validate_subject_token`, `invalid_grant`): a `ServiceProviderIdentity.not_deleted` row with a user for this client's own issuer, and carrying `dpop_jkt` when the client's record is `dpop_bound?`; the proof (`validate_public_client_proof`, `invalid_dpop_proof`), run only when the subject token carries a binding, with `ath` over the subject token and `expected_thumbprint: identity.dpop_jkt`; the sign-in live by the `OutOfBandSessionAccessor` TTL (`validate_session_live`, `invalid_grant`); identity assurance (`identity_verified?` from `User#identity_verified?` plus the sign-in's IAL) and an account that is not suspended (`invalid_grant`); the resource (`validate_resource`): a `TokenExchangeResourceServer` that is `usable?` and whose application `accepts_delegation_from?` the caller, every failure worded the same (`invalid_target`); the approval (`validate_grant`): `TokenExchangeGrant.live_by_application` and `valid_now?` with the current authorization taken from the grant's `rails_session_id` matching the identity's (`consent_required` naming `application.delegation_scope`). A `scope` parameter is not read.
4. Bearer and bound paths follow the subject token: an unbound subject token, which only a client without `dpop_required` can present, needs no proof and none is read, and the delegated token is issued as `Bearer`; a bound subject token is honored only with a proof from its key and the delegated token is bound to the same thumbprint, `token_type: DPoP` (`#issued_token_type_for(dpop_jkt:)`).
5. Registered format: `TokenExchangeResourceServer#saml?` decides what is issued; a `requested_token_type` naming the other format does not fail the exchange, the registered format is issued, `issued_token_type` reports it and `openid_connect_token_exchange` carries `requested_token_type_mismatch: true` beside `requested_token_type` and `token_type`.
6. Storage (hybrid): `DelegatedAccess::OpaqueToken.generate` (32 random bytes, base64url) and `.digest` (hex SHA-256) are the one source for every opaque string Login.gov hands out and looks up (`TokenExchangeToken.generate_token`, `TokenExchangeRefreshToken`, `DelegatedTokenStore.digest`, `Billing::SignInWaiverLink.digest`). `TokenExchangeToken#issue!` writes, in one transaction, the `token_exchange_tokens` issuance record (`grant_id`, `resource_server_id`, `service_provider_id`, `user_id`, `delegation_id`, `scope`, `ial`, `aal` from the identity or resolved from its `acr_values` through `AuthnContextResolver`, a fresh `refresh_family_id`, `token_type` `DPoP`, `Bearer` or `N_A`, `token_format`, `dpop_jkt`, `sp_rails_session_id` (`sensitive=true`), `issued_at`, `expires_at`, `revoked_at`, `revocation_reason`) and the grant's `first_exchanged_at` once; only after commit does `DelegatedTokenStore.write` put the live entry built by `TokenExchangeToken#live_attributes` in Redis at `delegated_token:<hex SHA-256 of the token>` (`aud`, `scope`, `grant_id`, `delegation_id`, `user_id`, `service_provider_id`, `resource_server_id`, `ial`, `aal`, `refresh_family_id`, `dpop_jkt`, `token_type`, `token_format`, `sp_rails_session_id`, `issued_at`, `expires_at`, `issuance_id`) with TTL equal to the lifetime, listed in `delegated_tokens:grant:<grant_id>` and `delegated_tokens:family:<refresh_family_id>`, whose TTL only ever grows. The table has no digest column; `DelegatedTokenStore.read` is the only validity check.
7. Lifetime: the lower of `token_exchange_access_token_ttl_seconds` (900) and the resource server's `max_access_token_seconds` (migration `20261009100600`), never higher; `expires_in` is the issuance record's `lifetime_seconds`.
8. Response (`#response`): `access_token`, `issued_token_type`, `token_type`, `expires_in`, `scope`; no `id_token`. On this branch's own diff the response carries no `refresh_token`, although the issuance record already carries the `refresh_family_id` the lifecycle branch attaches to; in the integrated stack the same method adds `refresh_token` and `refresh_token_expires_in` (seconds until the family ends, counted from `issued_at`) and `session_live: false` for a SAML assertion issued after the sign-in ended. Errors are 400 with `error` and `error_description`.
9. Re-approval and revocation: `TokenExchangeGrant.approve!` closes the earlier live rows as `superseded_by_new_consent` before the replacement is written, then each earlier row's `transfer_live_tokens_to!(replacement)` calls `DelegatedTokenStore.move_grant`, which rewrites every live entry's `grant_id` keeping its remaining TTL, moves the digests to the replacement's index set and re-points the open issuance rows (E97). `TokenExchangeGrant#revoke!` marks the row, calls `DelegatedTokenStore.revoke_grant` (deletes the listed entries and the set) and marks the open issuance rows with the same reason, so `user_revoked` and `sp_disconnected` end live tokens at once; `DelegatedTokenStore.revoke_family` serves the lifecycle branch.
10. Userinfo seam: `AccessTokenVerifier#load_identity` looks up `identities` only, and its comment states that delegated tokens live in `DelegatedTokenStore`, are never found there, and that no second lookup path is to be added; the userinfo request spec presents a live delegated token and gets 401, and the exchange spec shows a delegated token cannot be a subject token.
11. Removed: `OpenidConnect::ExchangeController`, the `/api/openid_connect/exchange` POST and OPTIONS routes and its `Rack::Cors` resource in `config/application.rb` (the token endpoint already carries the CORS rule a browser client needs); `IdTokenBuilder`'s `actor:` parameter and `act` claim (the builder is back to `main`'s shape, `c_hash` and `nonce` always present); minting through `IdentityLinker` against the target; the claim-space narrowing and the `target_in_use` check; the earlier exchange form body with its spec and the exchange controller spec.
12. Analytics: `openid_connect_token_exchange(success, service_provider_issuer, resource_server_identifier, application_issuer, client_type, token_type, requested_token_type, requested_token_type_mismatch, expires_in, error_code, error_details)`; the issuer is logged as authenticated or as claimed when authentication failed, and `sp_integration_errors_present` is fed with `event: :oidc_token_exchange_request` for the claimed issuer. Nothing refuses for volume.
13. Migrations: `20261009100600_add_max_access_token_seconds_to_token_exchange_resource_servers`, `20261009100700_create_token_exchange_tokens` (indexes on `grant_id`, `refresh_family_id`, `user_id`, `resource_server_id`, `service_provider_id`). Configuration: `token_exchange_access_token_ttl_seconds` (900) in `lib/identity_config.rb` and `config/application.yml.default`. Locale keys under `openid_connect.token.errors.*` (`client_not_approved`, `consent_required` with `%{delegation_scope}`, `invalid_requested_token_type`, `invalid_subject_token` and the other codes) in en, es, fr and zh.
14. Fixture: in `config/delegated_access.localdev.yml` MyBenefits Assistant is a public client (`pkce: true`, no `certs`, `dpop_required: true`); the records API carries `max_access_token_seconds: 300` as the read-write example.
15. Specs: `spec/requests/openid_connect/token_exchange_spec.rb` covers both client types end to end (issuance, userinfo refusal, no second exchange, revocation, every error class in the order above, the bearer and bound subject-token paths, replay of a client assertion and of a proof, header-only proof, flag off, other grant types); `spec/forms/openid_connect_unsupported_grant_form_spec.rb`, `spec/models/token_exchange_token_spec.rb`, `spec/services/delegated_token_store_spec.rb`, `spec/services/resource_server_authenticator_spec.rb`, `spec/services/delegated_access/opaque_token_spec.rb`, `spec/requests/openid_connect_userinfo_spec.rb`, `spec/factories/token_exchange_tokens.rb`; client assertions from `DelegatedAccessHelper` (fixture keys under `keys/`) and proofs from `DpopHelper`. Verified locally: 582 examples across the touched specs after the first four commits and 213 across the follow-up set (re-approval transfer, fixture); i18n spec 65 examples; rubocop and the analytics lints clean. Eleven commits on `delegated-access-dpop`.

## Decisions

- **D26** token storage is hybrid: a Redis live entry by digest plus a secret-free Postgres issuance record (full entry in section 16).
- **D27** delegated token lifetime is 900 seconds by default, shorter per API, never longer.
- **D28** the subject token's sign-in must be live for the first exchange; the family then runs independently.
- **D29** delegated tokens are opaque random references, never signed JWTs.
- **D30** exactly one `resource` per exchange; one token has one audience.
- **D31** `consent_required` naming the scope, only after authentication and for a registered resource; everything else `invalid_target`.
- **D37** no per-caller ceiling; every exchange and failure logged with issuer and error code.
- **D47** binding follows the service provider's record, never the API.
- **D57** the fixture service provider is a browser public client.
- **D70** the agency API's registered format decides the issued token type; `requested_token_type` is optional.
- **D71** an unknown grant type answers `unsupported_grant_type`.
- **D72** no scope narrowing on the exchange.
- **D82** the bearer exchange path for a public client whose record does not set `dpop_required`: `client_id` alone, no proof, bearer delegated tokens.

## How to review

- Diff against `delegated-access-dpop`.
- Check first the validation order in `OpenidConnectTokenExchangeForm` (nothing about the registry or approvals before the client is authenticated; the proof read only for a bound subject token), `TokenExchangeToken#issue!` (record in the transaction, Redis write after commit), the `DelegatedTokenStore` key and index-set shapes, and `ResourceServerAuthenticator` (signature before the replay cache).
- Specs that prove it: the exchange request spec (every error class in order, bearer and bound paths, replay of an assertion and of a proof, header-only proof, flag off, other grant types), the store and token model specs, the authenticator spec, the userinfo refusal.
- Must not change for existing clients: the `authorization_code` grant's responses are byte for byte the same; only a malformed `grant_type` changes its error text (an error path, D71); the token endpoint's CORS rule is unchanged.
- Known open items: at this branch's position an API registered `saml2` is refused as `invalid_target` and the refresh family it records is attached by the lifecycle branch; held until the harness run: `OpenidConnectTokenForm` delegating to `ResourceServerAuthenticator`, and `ServiceProviderIdentity#session_live?` as the one TTL test; the billing branch adds `Billing::DelegatedReturnRecorder` and the fraud-signals branch adds the token-issued event to `#issue!`.

# 7. `delegated-access-token-lifecycle`: renewing, ending and cleaning up delegated access

**Stack position** 7 of 14, on `delegated-access-token-exchange`. **Historical plan section** 5.5.

## Objective

A service provider that has exchanged once can keep working for the person without sending them back through Login.gov, for a bounded period, and can stop early. Every exchange opens a refresh family: the response carries a refresh token beside the delegated token, and the `refresh_token` grant at the existing token endpoint mints the next token for the same API, the same access and the same delegation identifier. The family ends at an absolute time, 12 hours after the exchange that opened it by default, never later than the person's remembered-approval period, and shorter where an agency or Login.gov has set a lower limit; a refresh never extends it. Continued access therefore rests on the family, not on the person's browser session, which is what lets a service provider finish an unattended task after the person has left.

The design follows the OAuth 2.0 security best current practice (RFC 9700) for refresh tokens. Every refresh rotates the credential under a database row lock, so two concurrent refreshes cannot both succeed; a refresh token presented a second time revokes the whole family and the theft is reported to the agency, so a stolen credential costs its holder the access rather than extending it. Only digests of refresh tokens are stored, and the delegated tokens themselves live in Redis for exactly their own lifetime. A refresh renews the one issuance record the exchange opened rather than writing another, so billing and the account page join a single record per exchange, and refresh-token rows are deleted a day after their family ends.

The branch also closes every path by which access could outlive the person's decision. Revoking an approval, by the person or by Login.gov, removes the family's live tokens at once and marks its rows, so no refresh can revive it; a service provider can end a family or a single token itself through the standard revocation endpoint (RFC 7009), which never confirms whether a token exists; account suspension and account deletion revoke every approval the person holds. Signing out of Login.gov touches nothing, because the person chose to let the service provider keep working.

## Functional requirements

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **FR-TOK-10** | Login.gov MUST allow the service provider to obtain a fresh delegated token for the same API without involving the person, for a bounded total period after issuance (12 hours by default, absolute, not extended by use). A refresh renews the issuance record the exchange opened (`refresh_count`, `last_refreshed_at`) rather than writing a new one, and refresh-token rows are purged a day after the family end (D79). |
| **FR-TOK-11** | The total period MUST never extend past the person's remembered-approval period, and for a non-remembered approval MUST end no later than 12 hours after issuance. A refresh renews the opening issuance record and refresh-token rows are purged a day after the family end, so the stored state of a family ends with the period (D79). |
| **FR-TOK-12** | Each renewal MUST retire the renewal credential used. Reuse of a retired renewal credential MUST end all access under that issuance and MUST be reported to the agency as a possible theft, so a stolen renewal credential ends access rather than extending it. |
| **FR-TOK-13** | A renewed token MUST have the same API, access, and delegation identifier as the original; the service provider MUST NOT be able to change them at renewal. |
| **FR-TOK-14** | The service provider MUST be able to end its access early (Token Revocation, RFC 7009). |
| **FR-TOK-15** | Continued access MUST NOT depend on the person's browser session with Login.gov remaining open. |
| **FR-TOK-16** | Login.gov MUST be able to set a shorter total period for a specific service provider or for agencies whose APIs can make changes, but never a longer one. |
| **FR-TOK-18** | Renewal credentials MUST be stored in a form from which the credential cannot be recovered. Delegated tokens MUST be stored the same way and held only for their own lifetime; the renewal credentials and a record of each issuance (which contains no credential) MUST be kept durably, so that revocation, reporting and the person's account page do not depend on a token still being live. |
| **FR-CEN-13** | Revoking an approval, by the person or by Login.gov, MUST immediately invalidate every token issued under it and MUST be reported to the agency (section 12). |

Rows owned by other sections that this branch carries a part of: **FR-TOK-20** (section 6; this branch carries the renewal half: no ceiling refuses a refresh, every refresh and failure is logged with the issuer and error code) and **FR-CEN-15** (section 3; this branch carries the suspension and deletion half: suspension revokes every approval with reason `account_suspended`, deletion from the account page or an account reset with `account_deleted`, sign-out touches nothing).

## Protocol rows

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **REF-1** | The exchange response (EXC-7) includes a `refresh_token` and a `refresh_token_expires_in` measured from the issuance instant (RFC 8693 §2.2.1 permits it). Refresh tokens are issued only by the exchange and only for delegated tokens. |
| **REF-2** | Service providers refresh at `POST /api/openid_connect/token` with `grant_type=refresh_token` (RFC 6749 §6), authenticating by client type: a confidential client with `private_key_jwt`, a public client with `client_id` and, when its record sets `dpop_required`, a DPoP proof from the family's key. The refresh token must belong to the service provider that performed the exchange, or the request fails with `invalid_grant`. |
| **REF-3** | The refreshed token has the same `aud`, `scope`, `act`, approval, `delegation_id` and format as the family. A `scope` parameter, if present, must equal the family's scope exactly or the request fails with `invalid_scope`. A `resource` parameter is refused with `invalid_request`. A family bound to a key (EXC-9) stays bound: the refresh carries a `DPoP` proof signed by that key, verified before the refresh token is looked up, and the refreshed token inherits the binding; a missing proof or a different key fails with `invalid_dpop_proof` without rotating the family. An unbound family is refreshed without a proof and its tokens stay bearer tokens (D82). |
| **REF-4** | Every refresh rotates the refresh token (RFC 9700 §4.14.2). Presenting a refresh token that has already been rotated revokes the entire family (all access and refresh tokens from that exchange) through `TokenExchangeRefreshToken.revoke_family!`, which marks the approval's refresh rows and issuance records for that family through `TokenExchangeToken.revoke_rows!`, reports `delegated-access-revoked` with `reason: refresh_token_reuse` to the family's API (ATT-7) and logs an analytics event carrying the service provider, the API and the family id, so operations see the reuse without reading agency events. Reuse, `approval_lapsed` and `client_revoked` all report this way. Reuse detection keeps every rotation of the family within its lifetime; keeping only the current and previous digest was considered and not adopted (D33, D79). |
| **REF-5** | Access tokens live 15 minutes (`token_exchange_access_token_ttl_seconds`, default 900). The refresh family has an absolute, non-sliding lifetime of 12 hours (`token_exchange_refresh_token_ttl_seconds`, default 43200) counted from the exchange that opens it, for every approval whether remembered or not, and never later than the approval's `remember_until`; a non-remembered approval's family does not end with the Login.gov session. An API's `max_family_seconds` and a service provider's `delegation_max_family_seconds` may lower the family lifetime, and an API's `max_access_token_seconds` may lower the access token lifetime; none may raise them (D32, D61). |
| **REF-6** | Delegated token validity does not depend on the person's Rails session. Sign-out at Login.gov does not revoke delegated families; account suspension or deletion does; approval revocation does. |
| **REF-7** | Refresh tokens are Postgres rows storing the SHA-256 digest only; the plaintext exists in the token response alone. Rows stay one per rotation while the family lives, for reuse detection. `ExpireDelegatedRefreshTokensJob` runs nightly and deletes the rows of a family one day after its `expires_at` (indexed); issuance records are not purged, their retention following `sp_return_logs` (D79). |
| **REF-8** | Login.gov exposes `POST /api/openid_connect/revoke` per RFC 7009 so a service provider can revoke a refresh token (and its family, reason `client_revoked`) or a single delegated token when the person's task ends early. The endpoint carries the token endpoint's CORS treatment so a public client revokes from the browser with its proof (D59). |
| **REF-9** | A refresh does not write a billing row (section 11) and emits `delegated-access-token-refreshed` (section 12) after the live entry is written. |
| **REF-10** | Rotation runs under a database row lock on the presented refresh token, so two concurrent refreshes with the same token cannot both succeed: the second sees `rotated_at` set and is treated as reuse. A family that is already revoked is not revoked or reported again on a further replay. |
| **REF-11** | A `resource` parameter on refresh is refused with `invalid_request` rather than ignored: the audience is fixed by the family, and a service provider that wants another API exchanges again. |
| **REF-12** | The refreshed access token's `expires_at` is the earlier of "now + access TTL" and the family's end, so no access token outlives its family. |
| **REF-13** | The refresh grant is not rate-limited per service provider. Refresh volume and error rate per service provider are monitored against configurable alert thresholds (D37). |
| **REF-14** | Storage split: live access tokens and SAML assertions in Redis (keyed by digest, TTL equal to the lifetime, indexed per approval and per family); refresh tokens and issuance records in Postgres. A refresh writes a new Redis entry and adds the new digest to the family's index set, but no new issuance row: the family keeps the one `token_exchange_tokens` row its exchange opened and the refresh updates that row's `refresh_count` and `last_refreshed_at`. The live entry carries the opening record's id, so introspection, revocation and billing join the same record for every token of the family. The earlier access token keeps its own TTL (D26, D79). |

## As built

1. `TokenExchangeRefreshToken` on `token_exchange_refresh_tokens` (migration `20261009100800`): `token_digest` (hex SHA-256, unique), `family_id` (the issuance record's `refresh_family_id`), `grant_id`, `token_exchange_token_id` (the issuance record the refresh token was handed out with), `resource_server_id`, `service_provider_id`, `user_id`, `scope` and `dpop_jkt` copied from the family, `expires_at` (the family's absolute end, the same value on every row of the family), `used_at` (last presentation, reuse attempts included), `rotated_at` (spent), `revoked_at`, `revocation_reason`; indexes on the digest, family, approval, issuance record, user, service provider and `expires_at` (migration `20261010100100`). `.lookup` refuses null bytes and finds by digest; `.live` is unrotated, unrevoked and before the family end; `#key_bound?` is `dpop_jkt` present; `#token_format` and `#saml?` read the issuance record.
2. `TokenExchangeRefreshToken.family_end(from:, grant:, resource_server:, service_provider:)` is the lowest of `token_exchange_refresh_token_ttl_seconds` (43200, `config/application.yml.default` and `lib/identity_config.rb`), `token_exchange_resource_servers.max_family_seconds` (migration `20261009100900`) and `service_providers.delegation_max_family_seconds` (migration `20261009101000`), counted from the exchange, never later than the approval's `remember_until`; a null column means the default and a value above the default has no effect. `TokenExchangeToken.lifetime_seconds_for(now:, resource_server:, family_expires_at:, token_format:)` is the single lifetime rule for every issued token: the lowest of the format's default TTL, `max_access_token_seconds` and the seconds left in the family, at least one second.
3. `DelegatedAccessClientHandling` (`app/forms/concerns/delegated_access_client_handling.rb`) carries `validate_client` for the exchange, refresh and revoke forms: a confidential client by `client_assertion` through `ResourceServerAuthenticator` with `aud` the endpoint being called, a public client by `client_id` naming a `pkce` record, the two credentials not interchangeable (`client_type_mismatch`), approval for delegation checked last (`client_not_approved`), every failure `invalid_client`; also `fail_with`, `error_code`, `error_response`, `url_options`, `validate_code_verifier_absent` (each form refuses `code_verifier` as `invalid_request` before the client is looked at) and `integration_errors` with a per-form `integration_error_event`. `OpenidConnect::DelegatedEndpointConcern` (`app/controllers/concerns/openid_connect/delegated_endpoint_concern.rb`) carries what the revoke and introspect controllers share: the `token_exchange_enabled` check (404 when off), the session-load, session-expiration and CSRF skips, the `options` action, `form_params` merging the `DPoP` header into the permitted body, and `log_integration_errors` feeding `sp_integration_errors_present`.
4. `OpenidConnectTokenExchangeForm#issue!` writes the issuance record, the family's first refresh row and the approval's `first_exchanged_at` in one transaction, then the Redis entry; the response carries `refresh_token` and `refresh_token_expires_in`, both measured from the issuance record's `issued_at`.
5. `OpenidConnect::TokenController#build_form` dispatches `grant_type=refresh_token` to `OpenidConnectRefreshTokenForm` while `token_exchange_enabled` is on (otherwise `OpenidConnectUnsupportedGrantForm`); the body permits `refresh_token` and `scope`. Checks in order, stopping at the first failure: `code_verifier` absent (`invalid_request`); the client (`invalid_client`); any `resource` refused (`invalid_request`, `resource_not_allowed_on_refresh`) and `refresh_token` present without null bytes (`invalid_request`); for a bound public client the proof for POST to the token URL with no `ath`, verified before the token is looked up (`invalid_dpop_proof`); the token found and this client's, else `invalid_grant` (`invalid_refresh_token`); the proof thumbprint equal to the family's `dpop_jkt` (`invalid_dpop_proof`, `dpop_key_mismatch`); `scope`, if given, exactly the family's (`invalid_scope`).
6. `#rotate_and_mint!` re-reads the row under `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` (`TokenExchangeRefreshToken.lock.find`). An unspent row is refused `invalid_grant` if revoked or past the family end; if the approval no longer stands (`approval_stands?`: not revoked, `current_content?`, a remembered approval still within `remember_until`; a single-authorization approval stands for as long as its family) the family is revoked with reason `approval_lapsed` and the refresh refused; if the API is not `usable?` or the application no longer `accepts_delegation_from?` the client, the refresh is refused and nothing is revoked, so switching the record back on restores the family. Otherwise `used_at` and `rotated_at` are set and `#mint!` renews the family's opening `token_exchange_tokens` record through `TokenExchangeToken#record_refresh!(now:)` (one `UPDATE` adding 1 to `refresh_count` and setting `last_refreshed_at`; migration `20261010100000`), writes the next refresh row (same family id, same `expires_at`, drawn from `DelegatedAccess::OpaqueToken`) and, after commit, the live entry from `TokenExchangeToken#live_attributes(issued_at:, expires_at:)` with the renewed times and the opening record's id as `issuance_id`, added to the family's index set with TTL the lifetime. The next token keeps the family's registered format and `issued_token_type` reports it; a SAML family's renewed assertion takes its `IssueInstant` and windows from the renewed times. `DelegatedAccessEvents.token_refreshed` is called after the live entry is written with the renewed expiry.
7. A spent row presented again has `used_at` updated and, unless the family is already revoked, `TokenExchangeRefreshToken.revoke_family!(family_id, grant:, reason: 'refresh_token_reuse')` runs: `DelegatedTokenStore.revoke_family` deletes the family's live entries and index set, `TokenExchangeToken.revoke_rows!` marks that approval's refresh rows and issuance records for the family (rows already revoked keep their reason), and `DelegatedAccessEvents.access_revoked(grant:, reason:, resource_server:)` reports to the one API the family was addressed to, so one theft is reported once. The answer is `invalid_grant` (`refresh_token_reused`).
8. Analytics: `openid_connect_token_refresh(success, service_provider_issuer, resource_server_identifier, application_issuer, client_type, token_type, family_id, reuse_detected, expires_in, error_code, error_details)`; `delegation_refresh_token_reuse(service_provider_issuer, resource_server_identifier, family_id)` when reuse is detected; `openid_connect_revoke(success, service_provider_issuer, client_type, token_type_hint, revoked, error_code, error_details)` with `revoked` one of `refresh_token`, `access_token`, `none`; `delegated_refresh_tokens_expired(deleted_count)`; `sp_integration_errors_present` fed with `oidc_token_refresh_request` and `oidc_revoke_request`. Nothing refuses for volume.
9. `POST /api/openid_connect/revoke` and its OPTIONS route; `OpenidConnect::RevokeController` (reads `DelegatedEndpointConcern`) and `OpenidConnectRevokeForm`: the client (`invalid_client`, 401), `token` present (`invalid_request`, 400), a bound public client's proof for POST to the revoke URL with `ath` over the token (`invalid_dpop_proof`, 400). `token_type_hint` only orders the two lookups. A refresh token of this client's, bound to the proving key when the family is bound, ends its family through `revoke_family!` with reason `client_revoked`; a live delegated token of this client's is removed alone through `DelegatedTokenStore.revoke_token` (entry and both index listings) and its issuance record marked `client_revoked`, the family untouched; the client's own sign-in access token, an unknown token, another client's, an already revoked one or one bound to another key is not acted on. Every authenticated outcome is 200 `{}` (RFC 7009 §2.2). `config/application.rb` gives the path the token endpoint's `Rack::Cors` treatment.
10. Cascades: `TokenExchangeToken.revoke_rows!(relation, reason:, now:)` marks every unrevoked row of a relation of issuance records or refresh tokens; `TokenExchangeGrant#revoke!` calls it for the approval's two relations and `#transfer_live_tokens_to!` re-points the refresh rows to the replacement approval so a refresh after re-approval issues under it. `TokenExchangeGrant.revoke_all_for_user!(user:, reason:)` revokes every live approval the person holds; `User#suspend!` calls it with `account_suspended`, `Users::DeleteController#delete_user` and `AccountReset::DeleteAccount#destroy_user` with `account_deleted` inside their transactions before the user row is destroyed (the approval, issuance and refresh rows stay as the record). Sign-out calls nothing.
11. `TokenExchangeRefreshToken.expired_for_purge` (`expires_at < 1.day.ago`) and `ExpireDelegatedRefreshTokensJob` (`queue_as :low`, `in_batches.delete_all`), scheduled with `cron_24h_and_a_bit` in `config/initializers/job_configurations.rb` as `expire_delegated_refresh_tokens`. A replay inside the day after the family end still revokes the family; after the purge it is refused as unknown.
12. Locale keys in en, es, fr and zh: `openid_connect.token.errors.invalid_refresh_token`, `refresh_scope_mismatch`, `refresh_token_missing`, `refresh_token_reused`, `resource_not_allowed_on_refresh`, `openid_connect.revoke.errors.token_missing`. Fixture: in `config/delegated_access.localdev.yml` the records API carries `max_family_seconds: 14400` beside `max_access_token_seconds: 300`, the read-write example.
13. Verified: `spec/requests/openid_connect/token_refresh_spec.rb` (75 examples), `spec/requests/openid_connect/token_exchange_spec.rb` (117), `spec/requests/openid_connect/revoke_spec.rb`, `spec/models/token_exchange_refresh_token_spec.rb`, `spec/models/token_exchange_grant_spec.rb`, `spec/models/user_spec.rb`, `spec/controllers/users/delete_controller_spec.rb`, `spec/services/account_reset/delete_account_spec.rb`, `spec/services/delegated_token_store_spec.rb`, `spec/jobs/expire_delegated_refresh_tokens_job_spec.rb`, the job-configuration spec and `spec/requests/openid_connect_cors_spec.rb`; the i18n spec (65 examples); the delegated-access set at the top of the stack 2,055 examples, 0 failures; rubocop, the analytics and tracker lints and gitleaks clean.

## Decisions

- **D26** Token storage is hybrid: live tokens in Redis by digest with per-approval and per-family index sets; refresh tokens and issuance records in Postgres.
- **D27** 15-minute delegated token lifetime by default, shortened per API by `max_access_token_seconds`, applied at every refresh.
- **D32** The refresh family's absolute lifetime is 12 hours for every approval, never past `remember_until`; ending with the Login.gov session and a 4-hour default were rejected.
- **D33** Refresh token reuse revokes the whole family, reports `refresh_token_reuse` to the agency and logs its own analytics event.
- **D37** No per-caller ceiling on refresh; volumes and error rates are monitored against alert thresholds.
- **D59** The revocation endpoint carries the token endpoint's CORS treatment so a public client revokes from the browser.
- **D61** The family clock starts at the exchange that opens the family; each exchange opens its own family.
- **D79** A refresh renews the opening issuance record (`refresh_count`, `last_refreshed_at`) and refresh-token rows are purged nightly a day after the family end; issuance records are kept as billing evidence.

## How to review

- Diff against `delegated-access-token-exchange`.
- Check first: `TokenExchangeRefreshToken.family_end` and `TokenExchangeToken.lifetime_seconds_for` (no token outlives its family, no column can lengthen a lifetime); `#rotate_and_mint!` under the row lock and its three refusal paths (revoked or ended, approval lapsed, API or application switched off, only the second of which revokes); the reuse path through `revoke_family!` (one report per theft, a family already revoked is not reported again); `#record_refresh!` (one issuance record per exchange, the live entry carrying the opening record's id); `OpenidConnectRevokeForm` outcomes (always 200 `{}` once authenticated, ownership and key binding checked before any action); `User#suspend!` and the two deletion call sites; the purge scope and the job's schedule.
- Specs that prove it: `token_refresh_spec.rb`, `revoke_spec.rb`, `token_exchange_spec.rb` (the refresh token on the exchange response), the refresh-token model spec, the grant, user and deletion specs, `expire_delegated_refresh_tokens_job_spec.rb`, the CORS spec.
- Must not change for existing clients: `grant_type=refresh_token` is dispatched to the delegated form only while `token_exchange_enabled` is on (otherwise `unsupported_grant_type` as before); the authorization-code grant's responses are unchanged; account deletion and suspension for a person with no approvals behave as before.
- Open items: a `User` deletion callback for the approval cascade in place of the two call sites, and `ServiceProviderIdentity#session_live?`, are held until the harness run (plan 6.1); the performance findings on the shared Redis pool, the per-address throttle and the ordering of Redis and database writes under partial failure are recorded in plan 6.3 and not implemented.

# 8. `delegated-access-introspection`: agency verification of delegated tokens

**Stack position** 8 of 14, on `delegated-access-token-lifecycle`. **Historical plan section** 5.6.

## Objective

A delegated token is an opaque reference that means nothing on its own, so an agency API confirms it with Login.gov before relying on it. The branch adds the standard Token Introspection endpoint (RFC 7662), `POST /api/openid_connect/introspect`. The agency API named as the token's audience authenticates with its own key (`private_key_jwt` against the certificates on its registry record) and receives, for a live token, everything it needs to act: who issued it, the person's identifier for that agency (the same pairwise identifier a direct sign-in produces, assigned if the person has never used the agency, without creating a connection), the approved access, the service provider acting (`act`), the assurance levels, when the person last authenticated, the token's times and identifier, and the delegation identifier shared by every token under the same approval. Together these are the elements NIST IR 8587 §5.2.1.1 requires of a token an agency relies on.

Identity attributes are released with the same confirmation, in the shape the agency already consumes from the user-information endpoint and limited to the attribute bundle registered for that agency, never chosen by the service provider. While the person's sign-in to the service provider is live the whole bundle is released; after it ends only the person's identifier, the delegation identifier and email are, and the response says so with `session_live`. The agency authenticates because the service provider also holds the token: a bearer call at the user-information endpoint would hand the service provider attributes the person never approved for it, which is why delegated tokens are refused there with or without a key-possession proof. A public-client service provider may ask about its own token, proving possession of the key the token is bound to, and learns only the token's status and what its own sign-in already conveys; every other caller receives a bare "not active" with no reason, so the endpoint cannot be used to probe tokens.

Login.gov publishes no cache window and imposes no per-agency ceiling: an agency verifies on every call, so a revocation takes effect at its next call, and volumes and error rates are monitored rather than refused.

## Functional requirements

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **FR-VER-1** | A delegated token MUST be verified with Login.gov before an agency relies on it. Login.gov MUST provide the means for the agency API to do so. |
| **FR-VER-2** | The agency MUST prove its identity to Login.gov when verifying a token. Login.gov MUST confirm a token and release the person's identity attributes only to the agency API the token was issued for. The service provider that holds the token MAY ask Login.gov about its own delegated token by proving possession of the key the token is bound to; it then learns only whether the token is valid, its validity period, its API, its access, its delegation identifier and key binding, the identifier for the person it already holds from its own sign-in, and any attribute the agency has explicitly chosen to share with the service provider, and no other identity attribute. Any other caller MUST learn nothing. |
| **FR-VER-3** | A confirmed token MUST report: who issued it (Login.gov), the person's identifier for that agency (the same one a direct sign-in produces, assigned if the person has never used that agency, without creating any connection to it), the approved access, the acting service provider, the assurance levels of the sign-in, when the person last authenticated to the service provider, issuance and expiry times, an identifier for this one token, and the delegation identifier shared by every token under the same approval. Together these are the elements NIST IR 8587 §5.2.1.1 requires of a token an agency relies on. |
| **FR-VER-4** | A confirmed token MUST also carry the identity attributes the agency is entitled to under its existing Login.gov agreement, in the same form a direct sign-in provides them, and nothing more. The set MUST be determined by the agency's agreement, never by the service provider. |
| **FR-VER-5** | Identity attributes MUST be available while the person's sign-in to the service provider is active. After it ends, verification MUST still confirm the token but MUST provide only the person's identifier, the delegation identifier and email, and MUST say so. |
| **FR-VER-6** | An invalid, expired, revoked, or foreign token MUST produce a plain "not valid" answer with no reason given. The same answer MUST be given to any caller that is neither the agency API the token was issued for nor the service provider proving possession of its own token. |
| **FR-VER-7** | Login.gov MUST NOT offer agencies a period during which an earlier confirmation may be relied on; an agency verifies the token on every call, so a revocation takes effect at the agency's next call. Login.gov's integration guide MUST say so. |
| **FR-VER-8** | Login.gov MUST NOT impose a hard per-agency ceiling on verification that refuses requests from a registered agency API. Login.gov MUST monitor verification volumes and error rates per agency API against configurable alert thresholds; protection against a runaway caller belongs to the infrastructure layer. |

Rows owned by section 6 that this branch gives effect to: **FR-TOK-5** (the person, the acting service provider, the approved access and the delegation identifier are what the agency's verification reads from the live entry) and **FR-TOK-6** (a delegated token is refused at the user-information endpoint with or without a key-possession proof; the agency's attributes come only through introspection; the documented opening for an agency that cannot yet verify by introspection is INT-17 below and is not built, D83).

## Protocol rows

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **INT-1** | Login.gov exposes `POST /api/openid_connect/introspect` per RFC 7662. Two kinds of caller authenticate. A resource server authenticates with `private_key_jwt` using the certificates on its `token_exchange_resource_servers` row; a failed assertion is 401 `invalid_client`. The public-client service provider that holds a delegated token may introspect it with `client_id` and a DPoP proof (RFC 9449 §4.3) whose `ath` is the hash of the `token` parameter and whose key thumbprint equals the token's `cnf.jkt`, no client assertion; a proof that is missing or fails on its own terms is 401 `invalid_dpop_proof` with `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP algs="ES256 RS256"`. A request with no credential, or a `client_id` that is not a public client approved for delegation, receives 200 `{"active": false}`. `/api/openid_connect/userinfo` refuses a delegated token with or without a proof: `AccessTokenVerifier#load_identity` resolves a token against `identities` alone and delegated tokens live in `DelegatedTokenStore`, so none is found there (EXC-6, EXC-18; D62, D83). |
| **INT-2** | The full active response is returned only when the caller is the token's resource server and: the live entry exists and `expires_at` is in the future; the approval is valid (CON-11, CON-12); the resource server is usable, the service provider is still approved for delegation; and the person's account is present and not suspended. When the caller is the service provider named in `act.sub` and the proof binds to the token, the response is the limited form of INT-14, under the same validity conditions. |
| **INT-3** | In every other case Login.gov returns exactly `{"active": false}` with HTTP 200 (RFC 7662 §2.2) and does not indicate why. The same answer is given when the service provider's proof verifies but its key is not the one the token is bound to; the key comparison is part of entitlement, never a reason given to the caller (D62). |
| **INT-4** | The active response to the resource server includes `iss`, `aud`, `scope`, `sub`, `act`, `acr`, `aal`, `exp`, `iat`, `jti`, `auth_time`, `client_id` (the service provider) and `delegation_id`. `jti` is the hex SHA-256 digest of the token, the key the live entry is stored under; it identifies the one token and says nothing about issuance volume (D63). `delegation_id` identifies the consent and is shared by every token in the refresh family. `auth_time` is when the person last authenticated to the service provider, the sign-in the delegation rests on, the same instant the SAML `AuthnInstant` carries. `acr` carries the identity-assurance context and `aal` the authentication-assurance context as `userinfo` reports them. `act` is always present on delegated tokens. For a key-bound token the response also includes `cnf: { jkt: <thumbprint> }` (RFC 9449 §6, RFC 7800), the key the resource server must verify a proof against on the request it received. |
| **INT-5** | `sub` is the person's `AgencyIdentity` uuid for the resource server's owning agency, computed as `userinfo` computes it (`AgencyIdentityLinker`) and created at first introspection if the person has never used that agency; no `identities` row for the agency application is created or read (EXC-5; D35). |
