| **INT-6** | The resource server's client assertion is validated per RFC 7523 §3 and RFC 8725 by `ResourceServerAuthenticator`: RS256 pinned, `iss` = `sub` = the resource server identifier, `aud` = the introspection URL, `exp` required and at most 5 minutes after `iat`, the signature verified before the `jti` is recorded through the shared replay guard so an anonymous party cannot burn a legitimate caller's `jti` values, and `jti` not previously seen within that window. |
| **INT-7** | Introspection is not rate-limited per resource server and never answers HTTP 429. Volume and error rate per caller are logged and monitored against configurable alert thresholds; runaway-caller protection is the infrastructure layer's (D37). |
| **INT-8** | Login.gov publishes no cache window. The integration guide states that a resource server introspects on every request and that revocation is therefore seen at the next request. `token_exchange_introspection_cache_seconds` is not introduced (D37). |
| **INT-9** | The active response includes `token_type` (`Bearer`, `DPoP` for a key-bound token, `N_A` for a SAML assertion) so a resource server can refuse a bound token presented with the wrong scheme. A failed credential answers HTTP 401 `invalid_client` (or `invalid_dpop_proof` for the service provider's proof, INT-1). |
| **INT-10** | The active response to the resource server also carries the identity claims the agency would receive from `userinfo` for a direct sign-in, with the claim names and formats `OpenidConnectUserInfoPresenter` produces (`email`, `email_verified`, `all_emails`, `given_name`, `family_name`, `birthdate`, `social_security_number`, `address`, `phone`, `phone_verified`, `verified_at`, `ial`, `aal`, `x509_*`), limited to the agency application's configured `attribute_bundle` and keyed by the agency's pairwise `sub`. Identity attributes come from the person's decrypted profile, which exists only while the service provider's Login.gov session is live (the same rule as SAML-5b). The member that states the session state is `session_live`, always present in the resource server's active response: `true` with the agency's bundle as written; `false` with `sub`, `delegation_id` and `email` (plus `email_verified`, and `all_emails` when bundled), the token members of INT-4 and no other identity claim. Delegated tokens remain unusable at `userinfo` (EXC-6; D34, D63). |
| **INT-11** | Claim release under INT-10 is decided by the agency application's bundle alone, never by the service provider's sign-in scopes: the person consented to the agency's bundle and to the service provider acting, not to the service provider choosing what the agency learns. `first_name` and `last_name` are filtered individually (OIDC's `profile:name` would release both); any address component in the bundle releases the whole composite `address` claim, as the SAML presenter already does. Claims absent from the bundle are omitted, never `null`; `locale` is never included. `auth_time` is not an identity claim and is carried as a token member (INT-4), not as part of the bundle. |
| **INT-12** | `ial` and `acr` carry the verified context only when the token's stored IAL is 2 (or IALmax) and the person holds an active profile; otherwise the auth-only context with no proofed claims, so a resource server must not assume proofed claims are present merely because `active` is true. `aal` falls back to the identity's requested AAL, then the default classref, exactly as `userinfo` reports. `attributes: "identifiers_only"` is not emitted; `session_live` (INT-10) is the one member that states whether the bundle or identifiers only were released (D63). |
| **INT-13** | `email` is the address the person chose to share with the service provider (the identity's shared email, else the last sign-in address); a delegated-only person has no identity row at the agency to hold a separate choice. `verified_at` is an integer epoch (userinfo format), while the SAML assertion carries ISO 8601; each protocol keeps its own convention (E24). |
| **INT-14** | The active response to the service provider (INT-1) carries `active`, `exp`, `iat`, `aud`, `scope`, `delegation_id`, `token_type`, `cnf`, `iss` and the service provider's own pairwise `sub` (the value its `id_token` carries), plus any claim the application lists in `service_providers.delegation_sp_shareable_attributes` (an application-level list, empty by default, maintained with the consent content) intersected with the agency's bundle. It carries no `act`, no `jti`, no `auth_time`, no `acr`/`aal`, no `session_live` and no other identity attribute from the agency's bundle (D36). |
| **INT-15** | Introspection reads the live token from Redis by the digest of the `token` parameter (`DelegatedTokenStore.read`; EXC-7 as the live entry); a missing entry is `active: false`. The issuance record is not consulted for validity (D26). |
| **INT-16** | Document images and document metadata (the `document_images` attribute set) are never released by introspection or in a SAML assertion, whatever the agency's bundle says; they are reachable only at `/api/openid_connect/userinfo` with the service provider's own access token, and delegated tokens are refused there (EXC-6, EXC-18). |
| **INT-17** (documented, not built) | The opening for an agency that cannot implement introspection in time, recorded so it can be built with full context, and recommended against (D83). (a) Opt-in per API on the registry record: `token_exchange_resource_servers.userinfo_allowed` (boolean, default false) with a mandatory `userinfo_allowed_until` date, set in `identity-idp-config` and in the Dashboard once it has the fields (D7), never by request parameter; `oauth` APIs only, since a `saml2` API is issued assertions. (b) At userinfo, a bearer token that resolves to no `identities` row but to a live `DelegatedTokenStore` entry whose resource server has the opening in force receives, as the userinfo JSON, exactly what introspection releases to that agency (`DelegatedTokenClaims`: `sub` the agency's pairwise identifier, `act`, the application's bundle while the sign-in is live and identifiers and email afterwards with `session_live`), with `aud` and `delegation_id`; in every other case the refusal of today, with or without a proof. (c) The entry must be live, unrevoked and unexpired by the one Redis read (INT-15) and meet the validity conditions of INT-2, so revocation keeps propagating. (d) `openid_connect_bearer_token` gains `delegated: true` and the resource server identifier; an alert on any such use is recommended. (e) UINF-1 does not apply: the caller is the agency, not the service provider the token's identity belongs to, and an agency with a registered certificate can implement introspection with the same key; no encryption variant is designed. (f) `ServiceProviderSeeder` and `ServiceProviderUpdater` warn when `userinfo_allowed` is set without a date or past it; the opening lapses at the date. (g) Unchanged: the service provider's own tokens at userinfo (plain and UINF-1), DPoP enforcement for bound tokens (EXC-18), the refusal of delegated tokens for every API without the opening, the introspection path and its responses. |

Rows about the user-information endpoint that belong elsewhere: **UINF-1**, **UINF-2** and **UINF-3** (section 14) and **DOC-1** (section 15, the direct-service-provider document-images feature kept on the base).

## As built

1. Routes `POST /api/openid_connect/introspect` and its OPTIONS; `OpenidConnect::IntrospectController` reads `OpenidConnect::DelegatedEndpointConcern` (404 while `token_exchange_enabled` is off, no session or CSRF, `options`, the proof read from the `DPoP` header only, integration-error logging) and keeps only its `create` action and permitted parameters. `config/application.rb` gives the path the token endpoint's `Rack::Cors` treatment.
2. `OpenidConnectIntrospectForm` includes `DelegatedAccessClientHandling` with `client_key_source` `:resource_server`, `confidential_client_authenticated(record)` keeping the authenticated `TokenExchangeResourceServer`, and `integration_error_issuer` and `integration_exists?` attributing a failed call to the resource server identifier or to the service provider that named itself. Callers: a `client_assertion`, or its type alone, means a resource server (`ResourceServerAuthenticator`, `key_source: :resource_server`, `aud` the introspect URL; failure 401 `invalid_client`); a bare `client_id` naming a `pkce` record that is `delegation_service_provider?` means the service provider, whose `DPoP` proof for POST to the introspect URL with `ath` over `token` is then required (`DpopProofVerifier`; a missing proof or one failing on type, algorithm, signature, `htm`, `htu`, `iat`, `ath` or a replayed `jti` is 401 `invalid_dpop_proof` with `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP algs="ES256 RS256", error="…", error_description="…"`); a `client_id` naming a confidential or unknown client, or no credential, is 200 `{"active": false}` without the token being examined; a proof signed by a key other than the token's is also `{"active": false}`.
3. Validity (`#token_active?`): `DelegatedTokenStore.read(token)` (null bytes refused), the entry present and `expires_at` in the future, the user present and not suspended, the approval present and `valid_now?`, the resource server `usable?` (URL, application and agency active), the token's service provider still `delegation_service_provider?`. Entitlement (`#caller_entitled?`): the resource server equal to the entry's `resource_server_id`, or the service provider equal to the entry's `service_provider_id` with `dpop_jkt` present and equal to the proof thumbprint. The issuance record is not consulted.
4. Full response to the resource server: `active`, `iss` (the root URL), `aud`, `scope`, `client_id` (the service provider's issuer), `delegation_id`, `token_type`, `iat`, `exp`, `cnf: {jkt}` when bound, `sub` (`DelegatedTokenClaims#agency_sub`: `AgencyIdentityLinker.for(user:, service_provider: application, skip_create: false).uuid`), `act: {sub: <service provider issuer>}`, `jti` (`DelegatedTokenStore.digest`), `acr`, `aal`, `auth_time` (`identities.last_authenticated_at`, else `iat`), `session_live`, then the identity claims.
5. `DelegatedTokenClaims` (`app/services/delegated_token_claims.rb`) maps the application's `attribute_bundle` names to the OpenID Connect scopes that release the same claims (`BUNDLE_SCOPES`), builds every claim userinfo could produce and filters through `OpenidConnectAttributeScoper`; `first_name` and `last_name` one at a time, any address component the composite `address`, a claim whose source is unavailable omitted. `#session_live?` is true while the entry's `sp_rails_session_id` still has a positive TTL in `OutOfBandSessionAccessor`; then the whole bundle comes from the session's decrypted profile with `session_live: true`; afterwards `sub`, `delegation_id` and `email` (with `email_verified`, and `all_emails` when bundled) with `session_live: false`. `email` is `email_address_for_sharing`, else the last sign-in address; `verified_at` only for an IAL2 application; `x509_*` only in a live PIV/CAC session.
6. Limited response to the service provider: the common members plus `sub` (`AgencyIdentityLinker.new(identity).link_identity.uuid`) and the claims for `service_providers.delegation_sp_shareable_attributes` (migration `20261009110000`, string array, empty by default, seeded from the fixture key of the same name in `config/delegated_access.localdev.yml`) intersected with the application's `attribute_bundle`.
7. `OpenidConnectClaimsFormatter#identity_proofing_claims` and `#x509_claims` hold the formatting lifted out of `OpenidConnectUserInfoPresenter` (the `address` claim, E.164 `phone`, ISO 8601 `birthdate`, five-digit `postal_code`, `phone_verified`, `x509_*`); the presenter calls it, so userinfo and introspection produce one claim shape.
8. Analytics `openid_connect_introspect(success, caller_type, resource_server_identifier, service_provider_issuer, active, error_code, error_details)`; `sp_integration_errors_present` with `event: :oidc_introspection_request`. No cache guidance and no rate limit.
9. Userinfo key binding: `AccessTokenVerifier.new(header, dpop_proof:, http_method:, http_url:)` reads the `Bearer` and `DPoP` schemes; `#load_identity` looks up `identities` only, so a delegated token is never found, with or without a proof. `#verify_key_binding`: an identity without `dpop_jkt` is accepted under `Bearer` and refused under `DPoP` (`token_not_bound`); a bound identity presented as `Bearer` is refused (`bound_token_requires_dpop`), and under `DPoP` the proof is verified for the request's method and URL with `ath` over the token and `expected_thumbprint: identities.dpop_jkt`. A key-binding refusal sets `#www_authenticate` to `DPoP algs="ES256 RS256", error="invalid_token"` (or `invalid_dpop_proof`), which `OpenidConnect::UserInfoController` and `OpenidConnect::DocumentImagesController` put on their 401. A confidential client's bearer request is unchanged byte for byte. Locale keys `openid_connect.user_info.errors.bound_token_requires_dpop` and `token_not_bound` in en, es, fr and zh.
10. Verified: 307 examples across the touched specs (`spec/requests/openid_connect/introspect_spec.rb` 50, `spec/forms/openid_connect_introspect_form_spec.rb`, `spec/services/delegated_token_claims_spec.rb`, the formatter, verifier, userinfo, `spec/requests/openid_connect_cors_spec.rb`, `spec/services/delegated_access_seeder_spec.rb` and `spec/services/service_provider_seeder_spec.rb`); the i18n spec (65 examples); rubocop and the analytics lint clean.

## Decisions

- **D26** One Redis read by digest decides validity; the issuance record is never consulted.
- **D29** Delegated tokens are opaque references; agencies learn everything through introspection.
- **D34** Attributes are released by session state: the agency's bundle while the sign-in is live, identifiers and email afterwards, the response saying which.
- **D35** The agency's `sub` is the agency-level pairwise identifier, created if missing, with no `identities` row.
- **D36** Who may introspect: the audience with `private_key_jwt` gets the full response; the public client holding the token, with a proof from its key, gets the limited one; everyone else `active: false`.
- **D37** No per-caller ceiling and no cache window; agencies introspect on every call.
- **D59** The introspection endpoint carries the token endpoint's CORS treatment for the public client's own introspection.
- **D62** A proof that verifies but is signed by the wrong key, no credential, or a non-public `client_id` answers `{"active": false}`, not 401.
- **D63** `jti` is the token's hex SHA-256 digest; `session_live` is the member that states the session state.
- **D83** Delegated tokens stay refused at userinfo; the opening for agencies that cannot yet introspect is documented (INT-17, plan 5.18) and not built.

## How to review

- Diff against `delegated-access-token-lifecycle`.
- Check first: the caller rules in `OpenidConnectIntrospectForm` (which outcomes are 401 and which are `active: false`); `#token_active?` and `#caller_entitled?` (the key comparison inside entitlement, never a reason); `DelegatedTokenClaims` filtering (one claim at a time for names, composite `address`, nothing released empty, `session_live` always present for the agency); the limited response's member set (no `act`, `jti`, `acr`, `aal`, `auth_time`, `session_live`); `AccessTokenVerifier#load_identity` resolving `identities` only, with the comment not to add a second lookup path.
- Specs that prove it: `introspect_spec.rb` (50 examples), the introspect form spec, `delegated_token_claims_spec.rb`, the formatter spec, the userinfo request spec, the CORS spec.
- Must not change for existing clients: userinfo's response shape is unchanged after the formatter extraction (the presenter spec and the userinfo request spec pin it); a confidential client's bearer request to userinfo is byte-identical with or without a stray `DPoP` header; the endpoint is 404 with the switch off.
- Context for D83 (plan 5.18): the product owner confirmed on 2026-10-13 that agencies verify by introspection and that userinfo refuses delegated tokens. Agencies do not support introspection today and need time, so the design of a time-boxed, per-API opening (`userinfo_allowed`, `userinfo_allowed_until`, set only through identity-idp-config) is recorded in INT-17 with the recommendation against it: userinfo authenticates no caller, the agency holds no key a delegated token is bound to, so acceptance there is bearer acceptance, and after D82 some delegated tokens are already bearer tokens. If it is ever built it touches `AccessTokenVerifier#load_identity`, reuses `DelegatedTokenClaims` and `#token_active?`, marks the userinfo analytics event `delegated: true`, and reopens the fraud-signal privacy review, the NIST IR 8587 conformance summary and the security review. Revisit when an agency asks and cannot implement introspection in time (plan 6 item 20).
- Open items: the released `email` is the one shared with the service provider (INT-13) until decided otherwise; whether agencies need to require a particular assurance level of the service provider's sign-in; `DelegatedTokenClaims` exposing identity, email and profile data to the SAML builder is held until the harness run (plan 6.1); KMS decryption on the verification path and repeated certificate parsing are recorded in plan 6.3 and not implemented.

# 9. `delegated-access-saml-assertions`: SAML assertions as an issued token type

**Stack position** 9 of 14, on `delegated-access-introspection`. **Historical plan section** 5.9.

## Objective

Some agency APIs consume SAML rather than OAuth: they already trust Login.gov's SAML signing certificate and validate assertions locally, and cannot add introspection quickly. RFC 8693 was written for exactly this case, one security token service issuing whichever token format the target needs. For an agency API registered with `token_format: saml2`, the exchange and every refresh mint a signed SAML 2.0 assertion instead of an opaque token, returned in the same token response. The registered format decides: the service provider may name a format, but a request that names none or names the other format is served in the registered format, which the response states and Login.gov logs, rather than refused, because the format is a fact about the API that only the agency's registration knows.

The assertion is scoped to one API (`Audience` and `Recipient` are the API's identifier and nothing else), names the person by the same agency-level pairwise identifier introspection reports as `sub`, carries the agency's registered attribute bundle exactly as a direct sign-in would, and adds the delegation attributes `delegation_scopes`, `delegation_id` and `actor`. The acting party is an attribute, not SAML's Delegation Restriction condition, so an agency that is not delegation-aware still accepts the assertion while one that is can apply its own policy. The assertion is always signed with the current signing key and encrypted to the agency's registered certificate when one exists. Both validity windows are five minutes, so a revoked approval is dead at every validator within five minutes and the service provider refreshes for a fresh assertion; as with introspection, once the person's sign-in has ended the assertion carries identifiers and email only and the token response says so with `session_live: false`.

Everything else is unchanged: the request, client authentication, the `resource` parameter, the approval checks, the refresh family, revocation, billing and fraud-signal events all behave for a SAML family as for an OAuth one. The assertion is built through an adapter over the identity provider's own assertion builder, generalized in three places that are each a no-op for the browser sign-in flow and pinned byte for byte by a spec, so no second builder exists and the existing SAML flow cannot drift.

## Functional requirements

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **FR-TOK-9** | Login.gov MUST be able to issue a SAML 2.0 assertion instead of an OAuth token for an agency API that consumes SAML, with the same access, record-keeping, and revocation behavior. The format is decided by the agency API's registration: the format registered for the API named in the request is the format issued. The service provider MAY name a format on the request; a request that names none is served, a request that names the registered format is served, and a request that names another format is served in the registered format, which the response states and Login.gov logs, rather than refused. An assertion is valid for five minutes, is always signed, and is encrypted to the agency's registered certificate when one exists. When an assertion is issued after the person's sign-in has ended, the response MUST say so with the same member the agency's verification uses (D45, D70, D73). |
| **FR-VER-9** | For SAML, the agency verifies the assertion locally against Login.gov's published signing certificate; the assertion MUST carry the same information as FR-VER-3 and FR-VER-4, and MUST be encrypted to the agency's certificate when one is registered. |

Rows owned by other sections that this branch gives effect to: **FR-TOK-23** (section 5; a key-bound assertion carries the thumbprint as the `dpop_jkt` attribute, and after D82 an assertion issued to a bearer public client carries none), **EXC-21** (section 6; `requested_token_type` optional, the registered format authoritative) and **FR-TOK-14** with **REF-8** (section 7; revocation by assertion `ID`).

## Protocol rows

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **SAML-1** | `requested_token_type` is OPTIONAL on the exchange (EXC-21). The resource server's registered `token_format` decides: `saml2` mints a SAML 2.0 assertion, `oauth` an OAuth token, whatever the request names or omits; a value that is neither `urn:ietf:params:oauth:token-type:access_token` nor `urn:ietf:params:oauth:token-type:saml2` fails with `invalid_request` (D70). |
| **SAML-2** | The response carries the base64url-encoded assertion without padding (not a `<samlp:Response>` wrapper; RFC 8693 §3 defines `saml2` as a base64url-encoded SAML 2.0 assertion) in `access_token`, `issued_token_type: urn:ietf:params:oauth:token-type:saml2`, `token_type: N_A`, `expires_in`, `scope`, `refresh_token` and `refresh_token_expires_in` per section 7, and `session_live: false` when SAML-5b applies. |
| **SAML-3** | The registered format is authoritative and a mismatch is not an error: a `saml2` request for an `oauth` API receives an OAuth token, an `access_token` request for a `saml2` API receives an assertion, `issued_token_type` states what was issued and the exchange analytics record the requested value (D70). |
| **SAML-4** | `Conditions/@NotBefore` is issuance − 5 s and `Conditions/@NotOnOrAfter` and `SubjectConfirmationData/@NotOnOrAfter` are both the issuance record's lifetime from its `issued_at` (`token_exchange_saml_assertion_ttl_seconds`, 300, capped by the API's `max_access_token_seconds` and the family end), so no validator accepts an assertion past five minutes and the revocation delay of SAML-10 is bounded by the same figure. The assertion has: `Issuer` = Login.gov's SAML issuer; `Subject/NameID` in persistent format = the person's pairwise identifier for the agency; a bearer `SubjectConfirmation` with `Recipient` = the resource server identifier; `Conditions/AudienceRestriction/Audience` = the resource server identifier and nothing else; an `AuthnStatement` whose `AuthnContextClassRef` is the verified-identity ACR and whose `AuthnInstant` is the service provider sign-in's authentication time (the issuance instant if none). No `InResponseTo` (D45). |
| **SAML-5** | The `AttributeStatement` follows the existing SAML pattern: the attributes `AttributeAsserter` produces for the agency application's configured attribute bundle, exactly what that agency receives when the person signs in to it directly (`uuid`, `ial`, `aal`, and its bundled identity attributes such as email, name, address, date of birth, SSN where bundled), with `uuid` the agency-level identifier and `email` the address the person shares with the service provider, plus the delegation attributes `delegation_scopes` (the token's scope), `delegation_id` and `actor` (the service provider's issuer, the SAML counterpart of the OAuth `act` claim), and `dpop_jkt` for a key-bound family only (FR-TOK-23; a bearer public client's assertion carries none, D82). Nothing outside the agency application's bundle is ever included. |
| **SAML-5b** | Identity attributes come from the person's decrypted profile, which Login.gov holds only in the person's live session (`OutOfBandSessionAccessor#load_pii`, the same path `userinfo` uses). An assertion minted or refreshed while the service provider's Login.gov sign-in is live includes the bundled identity attributes. One minted once that sign-in has ended includes only `uuid`, `ial`, `aal`, `email`, `all_emails` and the delegation attributes, and the exchange or refresh response carries `session_live: false`, the member introspection uses (INT-10), present only then, so the service provider can send the person back through Login.gov if it needs attributes again (D73). |
| **SAML-6** | The assertion is always signed with the current `SamlEndpoint` key using the existing signature options, and is encrypted to the resource server's registered certificate when one is present, through the existing encryption path (SAML-13 governs the algorithm) (D45). |
| **SAML-7** | Every issued assertion is recorded as an issuance record in `token_exchange_tokens` with `token_format: saml2`, `token_type: N_A` and the same `aud`, `scope`, approval, `delegation_id`, actor and `expires_at` as an OAuth token; there is no `token_digest` column. The SHA-256 digest of `Assertion/@ID` keys the live Redis entry (`DelegatedTokenStore`), with TTL equal to the window; the assertion XML is never stored. Revocation (section 7) applies unchanged (D26). |
| **SAML-8** | `token_exchange_resource_servers.token_format` (`oauth` default, `saml2`) decides the issued format (SAML-1). SAML consumers validate assertions locally; no introspection is required or expected of them, though introspection by assertion `ID` remains available to any agency that wants it. |
| **SAML-9** | A refresh (section 7) re-issues an assertion with a new `ID`, `IssueInstant` and `NotOnOrAfter` when the family's issuance record carries `token_format: saml2`, does not change `Audience` or `delegation_scopes`, and re-checks the approval (so a revoked approval stops at the next refresh). Revocation (RFC 7009) accepts the assertion `ID`. With the five-minute window a service provider that works continuously refreshes every five minutes, up to 144 assertions over the 12-hour family; no rate limit refuses this (D37). |
| **SAML-10** | Each agency declares at onboarding whether its API treats a bearer assertion as single-use (SAML Profiles §4.1.4.5 replay protection; the reference resource server's default) or accepts reuse within the subject-confirmation window. A service provider treats assertions as single-use unless the agency says otherwise, refreshing before each call. A revoked approval's assertion already delivered to the API remains usable until its window ends, at most five minutes; revocation otherwise takes effect at the next refresh. |
| **SAML-11** | The assertion's `Issuer` equals the metadata `entityID` (`SamlIdp.config.base_saml_location`), the embedded `X509Certificate` is byte-identical to the metadata certificate, and no Condition type other than `AudienceRestriction` is emitted, because `ruby-saml`-based validators reject each of those. |
| **SAML-12** | Revocation and introspection accept either the assertion `ID` exactly as it appears in the XML (leading underscore, since that value is what is digested) or the encoded plaintext assertion as `token` (`DelegatedSamlAssertion.reference_for`). Revocation by either ends that assertion only, as for an opaque access token; the family is revoked by its refresh token. An encrypted encoded assertion cannot serve as the `token` because its ID is inside the ciphertext; the service provider revokes such a family by refresh token, or the assertion by the ID the resource server reads after decrypting (D69). |
| **SAML-13** | Encryption follows the agency application's `block_encryption` when it encrypts responses and `aes256-cbc` otherwise, with `rsa-oaep-mgf1p` key transport, through the existing encryption path; a registered certificate always means an encrypted assertion, and onboarding validates the certificate. |
| **SAML-14** | Identifiers-only (SAML-5b) keeps `uuid`, `ial`, `aal`, `email`, `all_emails` and the delegation attributes; `verified_at` is dropped with the bundle; `locale` and x509 attributes are never emitted. `ial` and `aal` attributes use the legacy classref form the agency already receives at direct sign-in, while `AuthnContextClassRef` is the verified ACR. The token response states the case with `session_live: false` and no other member (D73). |
| **SAML-15** | The assertion is built inside the issuance-record transaction, so a signing or encryption failure (for example an unparseable resource-server certificate) leaves no record and no live entry; it surfaces as a server error rather than a token-endpoint error code. The prepended gem extension copies `AssertionBuilder#fresh`; a regression spec compares browser-flow output with the gem's method byte for byte so a `saml_idp` upgrade fails loudly instead of drifting silently. |

## As built

1. `lib/saml_idp_extensions/assertion_builder.rb`, prepended to `SamlIdp::AssertionBuilder` from `config/initializers/saml_idp.rb`: `SubjectConfirmationData/@InResponseTo` is emitted only when there is a request id (the gem writes an empty one otherwise, which relying parties reject); `subject_confirmation_expiry:` sets the bearer window in place of the gem's fixed three minutes; `issue_instant:` pins `IssueInstant` so it and both `NotOnOrAfter` values agree to the second with the issuance record. `fresh` is the gem's method with one line changed and `raw` re-aliased to it; `spec/lib/saml_idp_extensions/assertion_builder_spec.rb` compares browser-flow output with the gem's own method byte for byte.
2. `DelegatedSamlAssertion` (`app/services/delegated_saml_assertion.rb`, `#initialize(issued_at:, lifetime_seconds:)`) is an adapter over the builder fed what an AuthnRequest and the session normally supply: `Issuer` the metadata entityID; persistent `NameID` from `DelegatedTokenClaims#agency_sub`; a bearer `SubjectConfirmation` with `Recipient` the resource server identifier and no `InResponseTo`; `Audience` the same identifier; both `NotOnOrAfter` values the issuance record's lifetime from `issued_at`; `AuthnInstant` the sign-in's `identities.last_authenticated_at`; `AuthnContextClassRef` the verified-identity ACR. The attribute statement is `AttributeAsserter`'s output for the agency application's bundle (the asserter accepting `authn_request: nil` with the issuance record's `ial` and `aal`), `uuid` re-pointed at the agency identifier, `email` at the shared address, `locale` dropped, plus `delegation_scopes`, `delegation_id`, `actor` and, when `issued.key_bound?`, `dpop_jkt`. While `DelegatedTokenClaims#session_live?` is true the decrypted profile is read through `OutOfBandSessionAccessor#load_pii`; otherwise `#identifiers_only?` is true and only `uuid`, `ial`, `aal`, `email`, `all_emails` and the delegation attributes remain. Signed with the current `SamlEndpoint` key; encrypted to `resource_server.ssl_certs.first` when registered (`#encryption_opts`: the application's `block_encryption` or `aes256-cbc`, `rsa-oaep-mgf1p`), `builder.encrypt(sign: true)`; returned signed in the clear when no certificate is registered.
3. Issuance in `OpenidConnectTokenExchangeForm#issue!` when `resource_server.saml?`, whatever `requested_token_type` says or omits: the issuance record carries `token_format: saml2` and `token_type: N_A` for both client types; the assertion is built inside the transaction; `DelegatedTokenStore` is keyed by the SHA-256 digest of the assertion `ID` (`_<uuid>`, `DelegatedSamlAssertion.new_assertion_id`, chosen before the record is written) with TTL equal to the window; the XML is never stored. `TokenExchangeToken.lifetime_seconds_for(token_format:)` takes `token_exchange_saml_assertion_ttl_seconds` (300, `lib/identity_config.rb` and `config/application.yml.default`) as the default for `saml2`, capped by `max_access_token_seconds` and the family end. Response: `access_token` the base64url assertion, `issued_token_type` the saml2 URN, `token_type: N_A`, `expires_in`, `scope`, `refresh_token`, `refresh_token_expires_in`, and `session_live: false` only when `@assertion.identifiers_only?`.
4. Refresh in `OpenidConnectRefreshTokenForm#mint!` re-issues what the exchange issued: `TokenExchangeRefreshToken#token_format` and `#saml?` read the issuance record, a SAML family gets a new assertion with a new `ID`, `IssueInstant` and windows from the renewed times for the same audience and access after the usual approval checks, `token_type` is copied from the record, and the response carries `issued_token_type` saml2 and `session_live: false` once the sign-in has ended.
5. `DelegatedSamlAssertion.reference_for(presented)` returns the `ID` of a base64url- or base64-encoded plaintext `<saml:Assertion>` (at most 64 KB, parsed strictly without network access, root element and namespace checked) and otherwise the presented value itself, so `OpenidConnectIntrospectForm` and `OpenidConnectRevokeForm` accept an assertion `ID` or the encoded assertion as `token`; an encrypted assertion hides its ID and is answered not active or not acted on. Introspection reports `token_type: N_A`, `cnf.jkt` for a bound family and `jti` the digest of the ID. Revocation by ID or encoded assertion ends that assertion alone (`client_revoked`).
6. Removed: the `saml_not_available` refusal of the exchange branch; `REQUESTED_FORMAT_MUST_MATCH_RESOURCE`, the mismatch branch of `validate_requested_token_type`, the `requested_token_type_mismatch` strings and the `attributes: "identifiers_only"` member, in en, es, fr and zh. No migration: `token_format` on `token_exchange_resource_servers` and `token_exchange_tokens` comes from the registry and exchange branches.
7. Fixture: the SAML API in `config/delegated_access.localdev.yml` (`https://benefits-api.agency.localdev`, `token_format: saml2`) keeps its encryption certificate; the assertion is encrypted when the certificate file is present locally and returned signed in the clear when it is not.
8. Verified: 594 examples across the touched specs. `spec/requests/openid_connect/saml_assertion_exchange_spec.rb` covers both client types end to end (the encrypted and signed assertion checked as a relying party checks it, the attribute set with `dpop_jkt` for a bound public client, the configured window, refresh with identifiers only after the sign-in ends, introspection by ID and by encoded assertion, revocation by ID, by encoded assertion and by refresh token), with `spec/services/delegated_saml_assertion_spec.rb`, `spec/lib/saml_idp_extensions/assertion_builder_spec.rb`, `spec/services/attribute_asserter_spec.rb`, `spec/models/token_exchange_token_spec.rb` and `spec/requests/openid_connect/token_exchange_spec.rb`; rubocop clean.

## Decisions

- **D44** `requested_token_type` required and the service provider chooses the format (replaced by D70).
- **D45** Five-minute subject-confirmation and conditions windows; always signed; encrypted to the agency's registered certificate when one exists.
- **D69** Revoking a SAML assertion by ID ends that assertion only; the family is revoked by its refresh token.
- **D70** The agency's registered `token_format` decides the issued token type; `requested_token_type` is optional and a mismatch is served in the registered format and logged, not refused.
- **D73** An assertion issued after the sign-in ended is signaled with `session_live: false` on the token response, present only when false.
- **D82** Key binding is a per-client setting; an assertion issued to a bearer public client carries no `dpop_jkt` attribute.

## How to review

- Diff against `delegated-access-introspection`.
- Check first: the builder extension is a no-op for the browser flow (the byte-for-byte spec); the assertion's `Issuer`, `Audience`, `Recipient` and both `NotOnOrAfter` values against the issuance record; which attributes appear once the sign-in has ended and that `session_live: false` appears only then; `dpop_jkt` present for a bound family and absent for a bearer one; `reference_for` input limits (64 KB, strict parsing, no network); that `resource_server.saml?` alone decides the format and the response states it.
- Specs that prove it: `saml_assertion_exchange_spec.rb`, `delegated_saml_assertion_spec.rb`, `assertion_builder_spec.rb`, the attribute asserter spec, the token model spec.
- Must not change for existing clients: the SAML browser flow's assertions are byte-identical to the gem's own output; `AttributeAsserter` with an AuthnRequest behaves as before; the OAuth exchange and refresh responses for an `oauth` API are unchanged; a service provider itself signs in with OpenID Connect, and SAML is only the format issued for the agency API.
- Open items: revocation of an issued assertion is observed by the agency only at expiry, bounded to five minutes (D45); revoke-by-ID ending the assertion only, not the family, is recorded for review (D69); accepting the bare assertion `ID` as the RFC 7009 `token` is a Login.gov extension flagged for review (E14); `DelegatedTokenClaims` exposing identity, email and profile data to the builder so `DelegatedSamlAssertion` stops reading them itself is held until the harness run (plan 6.1); which agencies need SAML rather than OAuth, and whether any needs both, is unanswered.

# 10. `delegated-access-operations`: discovery metadata, monitoring, the fixture contract and the threat sweep

**Stack position** 10 of 14, on `delegated-access-saml-assertions`. **Historical plan section** 5.11.

## Objective

This branch makes the delegated-access capability observable and switchable as one unit, and it fixes the contract between the identity provider and the three reference applications that partners use to integrate. The discovery document (`/.well-known/openid-configuration`) advertises the token-exchange and refresh grants, the verification and revocation endpoints, the `none` client-authentication method for the browser public client and the key-possession proof algorithms only while the master switch `token_exchange_enabled` is on; with the switch off the document is, member for member, the one Login.gov published before this work, so a partner never discovers an endpoint that would answer `unsupported_grant_type` or 404 and nothing about delegation is advertised when Login.gov has turned it off.

Monitoring is done from the analytics stream rather than from refusals: every delegated-access event names the caller with one property (`service_provider_issuer` for a service provider, `resource_server_identifier` for an agency API), so one filter selects a caller across issuance, renewal, revocation, verification, consent and account-page events, and operations alarms apply volume and error-rate thresholds to that stream. No registered caller is refused for volume. The local fixture `config/delegated_access.localdev.yml` is the single contract between the seeder and the reference applications and the end-to-end harness: its header names the three applications, their fictitious operators and the variables that relocate them to a sandbox, and the seeder specification asserts everything they read from it, so a change to an issuer, a scope value or an API identifier is visibly a change to the reference applications. A threat sweep pins the cross-cutting refusals (foreign tokens, replayed proofs, bearer presentation of a bound token, delegated tokens at the user-information endpoint, exchange after revocation or deactivation) end to end on the integrated endpoints, and each refusal is checked for the analytics event operations alert on.

## Functional requirements

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **FR-OPS-2** | Login.gov MUST monitor issuance, renewal, verification and revocation volumes and errors, approved-but-unused delegations, fraud-signal delivery completeness and billing correlation. The identity provider's part is the analytics stream: every delegated-access event names the caller with the same property (`service_provider_issuer` or `resource_server_identifier`) and carries the RFC error code on a refusal; alert thresholds are deployment alarms over that stream, not application configuration, and no registered caller is refused for volume. Approved-but-unused delegations are reported by section 11 and fraud-signal completeness by section 12. |
| **FR-OPS-4** | Rollout MUST proceed from local and sandbox testing with reference applications, to one service provider and one agency in the sandbox, to fraud-signal and billing verification, to production, with a review gate at each step. The reference setup the identity provider carries is one fixture file that names the three reference applications and relocates them to a sandbox by environment variables, with the API identifiers unchanged by hostname. The service provider reference application is a browser-based public client that signs in with PKCE, holds its key-possession key in the browser only (non-extractable, a new key per sign-in) and makes the code exchange, token issuance, renewal, revocation and the agency API calls from the browser with key-possession proofs; its server serves static files and a non-secret configuration and holds no private key or shared secret (D57). The agency reference APIs and Login.gov's revocation and verification endpoints accept cross-origin browser calls so that client can reach them (D59; FR-TOK-14, FR-VER-6). |
| **FR-OPS-5** | Login.gov MUST provide working reference applications for both partner roles (service provider and agency API, OAuth and SAML) and an automated end-to-end test that partners can run against the sandbox. The harness reads issuers, API identifiers, scope values, redirect URIs and hostnames from the fixture file and from nowhere else (D51). The service provider reference is the browser public client of FR-OPS-4 (D57); fraud-signal viewer code is carried only by the OAuth agency reference, which had it before this project, and the SAML agency's events can be observed through it (D58). |

Rows owned by other sections and referenced here: **FR-OPS-1** (the master switch, section 1; this branch delivers its discovery half, DISC-5). **FR-OPS-3** and **FR-OPS-6** (security review and partner guides) are delivered outside the repository (section 15). The removal of the per-API key-binding column was built here and moved to section 5 (D64): **FR-TOK-22**, **FR-TOK-24**.

## Protocol rows

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **DISC-1** | `/.well-known/openid-configuration` advertises every new grant type and endpoint under the metadata names of OpenID Connect Discovery 1.0 and RFC 8414 §2: `grant_types_supported` adds `refresh_token` and `urn:ietf:params:oauth:grant-type:token-exchange`; `token_endpoint_auth_methods_supported` adds `none` beside `private_key_jwt` for the public-client service provider; `introspection_endpoint` with `introspection_endpoint_auth_methods_supported` (`private_key_jwt`, `none`) and `introspection_endpoint_auth_signing_alg_values_supported` (`RS256`); `revocation_endpoint` with the same two members. `none` appears on the introspection and revocation members because the public-client service provider calls both with `client_id` and a key-possession proof and no client authentication. |
| **DISC-2** | Discovery includes `dpop_signing_alg_values_supported` with `ES256` and `RS256` (RFC 9449 §5.1) while delegated access is enabled (D46). |
| **DISC-3** | No non-standard metadata key is added. There is no `token_exchange_endpoint`: RFC 8693 performs the exchange at the token endpoint, which is already advertised. |
| **DISC-4** | `scopes_supported` remains the static list of standard scopes. `token_exchange:*` values are per partner and are not enumerated in discovery; the developer documentation describes the prefix. |
| **DISC-5** | Every member above appears only when `token_exchange_enabled` is true, so the document never advertises something that returns `unsupported_grant_type` or 404; with the switch off the document equals the pre-delegation document member for member and in key order (D48). |

## As built

1. `OpenidConnectConfigurationPresenter` adds, only while `token_exchange_enabled` is on: `refresh_token` and `OpenidConnectTokenExchangeForm::GRANT_TYPE` in `grant_types_supported`; `none` in `token_endpoint_auth_methods_supported`; `introspection_endpoint` and `revocation_endpoint`, each with `*_auth_methods_supported` (`private_key_jwt`, `none`) and `*_auth_signing_alg_values_supported` (`RS256`); `dpop_signing_alg_values_supported` from `DpopProofVerifier::ALLOWED_ALGORITHMS`. No `token_exchange_endpoint`; `scopes_supported` static. `spec/presenters/openid_connect_configuration_presenter_spec.rb` (11 examples) holds the off-state document as a literal and asserts member and key-order equality.
2. One caller property across the delegated-access analytics events: `delegation_consent_submitted` (`app/controllers/sign_up/completions_controller.rb`), `delegation_account_approved` (`app/controllers/accounts/delegated_access/approvals_controller.rb`) and `delegation_account_revoked` (`app/controllers/accounts/delegated_access/revocations_controller.rb`) log `service_provider_issuer`, as `openid_connect_token_exchange`, `openid_connect_token_refresh`, `openid_connect_revoke` and `openid_connect_introspect` do; agency callers are named by `resource_server_identifier`. Docstrings in `app/services/analytics_events.rb`.
3. Alert thresholds live in the deployment's alarms over the analytics stream, per caller over a one-minute window: error rate 5 percent on the four token events, 1000 exchanges, 6000 introspections and 1000 refreshes per minute. No `token_exchange_alert_*` key exists in `lib/identity_config.rb` or `config/application.yml.default`; nothing in the application refuses a caller for volume.
4. Fixture contract: the header of `config/delegated_access.localdev.yml` names the three reference applications and their fictitious operators, with the variable that relocates each (`DELEGATION_SP_URL` for the browser public client MyBenefits Assistant of the Office of Benefits Coordination on :9292; `DELEGATION_OIDC_AGENCY_URL` for Housing Assistance Records of the Department of Housing Support, OAuth API `https://records-api.agency.localdev`, scope value `housing_records`, on :9393; `DELEGATION_SAML_AGENCY_URL` for Retirement Benefits Portal of the National Retirement Administration, SAML API `https://benefits-api.agency.localdev`, scope value `retirement_benefits`, on :4567). `spec/services/delegated_access_seeder_spec.rb` asserts the service provider's issuer, friendly name, agency, IAL2, `pkce` with no certificates and no Attempts API credentials, its three redirect URIs and its per-client `dpop_required: true`; each application's agency, scope value, accepted service providers, API identifier (an RFC 8707 resource indicator, unchanged by hostname), token format and active state; relocation of all three hosts, redirect URIs, ACS URL and learn-more URLs from the environment; and that no API entry carries a `dpop_required` key.
5. Threat sweep: `spec/requests/openid_connect/delegated_access_threats_spec.rb` (13 examples) on the integrated endpoints: a subject token belonging to another client in both directions; a key-bound sign-in token presented as a bearer token at the user-information endpoint (refused with the `DPoP` challenge, accepted with the proof); a delegated token at the user-information endpoint with and without a proof; a replayed `jti` at the same endpoint and at another; a proof for another URL; an expired proof at the exchange and at the user-information endpoint; a refresh with a key other than the family's (family intact for the right key); introspection by an API the token was not issued for (`active: false`); an exchange after the approval was revoked, after Login.gov withdrew its approval of the service provider (`invalid_client`), and after the API URL or application was deactivated (`invalid_target`), each with the earlier token dead. Every refusal is checked for its analytics event, caller property and `error_code`.
6. Migrations: none. Configuration: `config/delegated_access.localdev.yml` header and contract. Verification: threat sweep 13, presenter spec 11, the touched specs together 116 (presenter, seeder, the three controller specs, the sweep), i18n spec 65; rubocop and the analytics-events lint clean.

## Decisions

- **D37** Monitoring thresholds alert; nothing refuses a registered caller for volume (full entry in section 16).
- **D46** Key-possession proof algorithms are `ES256` and `RS256`, advertised in discovery.
- **D48** Discovery advertises the delegation metadata only while `token_exchange_enabled` is on.
- **D51** One fixture file is the contract between the seeder and the end-to-end harness.
- **D57** The service provider reference application is a browser-based public client.
- **D58** Fraud-signal viewer code stays only in the OAuth agency reference.
- **D59** Cross-origin browser calls are accepted at the agency reference APIs and at revocation and verification.
- **D64** The discovery members stay on this branch because the presenter names routes that exist only from the lifecycle and introspection branches on; the per-API key-binding removal moved to section 5.

## How to review

- Diff against `delegated-access-saml-assertions`.
- Check first: the presenter's off-state literal equals the document Login.gov publishes today, member for member and in order; the on-state members against RFC 8414 §2 and RFC 9449 §5.1; the fixture header against what the three reference applications read.
- Then read the threat sweep as the integrated acceptance test for sections 5 to 9: each example names the requirement it pins and the analytics event operations alert on.
- Specs that prove it: `spec/presenters/openid_connect_configuration_presenter_spec.rb` (11), `spec/requests/openid_connect/delegated_access_threats_spec.rb` (13), `spec/services/delegated_access_seeder_spec.rb` (the contract examples), the three controller specs for the property rename.
- Must not change for existing clients: with the switch off the discovery document is unchanged; the analytics rename touches only the three delegated-access events; `scopes_supported` is unchanged in both states.
- Known open items: the browser client's scope names were out of contract with `token_exchange:housing_records` and `token_exchange:retirement_benefits` and the client, not the fixture, is being corrected; the passport agency's direct-service-provider record for the document-images allow-list is not in the fixture (plan 5.12, section 15); four pre-existing environment failures in the full suite (three browser specs needing the sample service provider's port, one layout spec needing undigested asset names) recur on every branch above; FR-OPS-3 and FR-OPS-6 are outside this repository.

# 11. `delegated-access-billing-reporting`: billing delegated use to the agency and reporting on delegation outcomes

**Stack position** 11 of 14, on `delegated-access-operations`. **Historical plan section** 5.8.

## Objective

This branch bills delegated use to the agency whose API was used, in the same records and reports direct use already flows through, and gives Login.gov a monthly view of what was requested, approved and used. When a service provider exchanges its sign-in for a delegated token, the exchange writes one billing row (`sp_return_logs`) under the API's billing issuer, marked `delegated`, at the identity assurance level the sign-in is billed at; the first exchange under an approval is billable and later ones are a non-billable trail; renewals write nothing. The row carries one new column only. The acting service provider, the API and whether the person verified identity in that sign-in are read by join from the token issuance record through an append-only adjustment row, so the invoice history stays reproducible and the billing table is never rewritten.

The service provider is billed for its own sign-in by default, like any customer, and that sign-in is waived when a delegated token is issued for it, because the agencies receiving the tokens are billed instead. The waiver is never a mutation: a Redis link written at the handoff (keyed by the digest of the service provider's access token, held for one hour) or, when it has lapsed, a lookup in Login.gov's own records finds the sign-in row, and an adjustment row excludes it from the invoice queries. The exchange is never refused or delayed for billing. Because billing work in this repository has a data team that owns the invoice rules, the rules in the repository billing skill govern where documents disagree, and the items the data team has not yet answered stand as built (D75) and are listed under How to review.

## Functional requirements

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **FR-BIL-1** | Each delegated use MUST be attributed to the agency whose API was used, at the identity assurance level of the person's sign-in, and recorded in the same billing record as direct use, marked as delegated. The billing record carries only the delegated marker; the acting service provider, the API and whether the person verified identity in that sign-in are recorded on the token issuance record the billing record points to, and reports read them from there (D40). |
| **FR-BIL-2** | The first use under each approval per sign-in MUST be billable; later uses and renewals MUST be recorded but not billable. |
| **FR-BIL-3** | A person who uses an agency both directly and through delegation in one month MUST be billed to that agency once. |
| **FR-BIL-4** | Existing invoices and reports MUST include delegated use in their totals and SHOULD add a direct/delegated breakdown. |
| **FR-BIL-5** | Login.gov MUST produce a monthly delegation outcomes report, per service provider and agency: delegations requested; withdrawn before use (the nearest recorded refusal, since requested applications are take-it-or-cancel and a cancelled screen writes no record); approved but never used; and used. It MUST also report identity verifications that occurred in a service provider sign-in that led to no billable agency use (D68). |
| **FR-BIL-6** | The record of which service provider initiated a person's identity verification MUST NOT be changed by delegation. How verification cost is shared between the service provider's and the agency's agreements is a reporting rule, not runtime behavior. |
| **FR-BIL-7** | Billing MUST NOT rely on storing token values. |
| **FR-BIL-8** | The service provider is billed for its own sign-in by default, like any customer. When at least one delegated token is issued for that sign-in, the sign-in MUST become non-billable, because the agencies receiving the tokens are billed instead; this MUST happen together with the first agency charge so that no sign-in is ever billed twice or to no one. A sign-in for which no delegated token is ever issued stays billable to the service provider. A consent screen the person cancels completes no sign-in and is billed to no one, as for every service provider today. Reports MUST show, per service provider and month, how many sign-ins were billed and how many were waived because tokens were issued. When the person verified identity during such a sign-in, every agency that received a delegated token is billed for it, the identity verification included; the service provider is not billed for that sign-in (D43). The waiver is recorded as a separate adjustment, never by changing the sign-in's record; the link from the sign-in to the exchange is held for one hour (D41), and when it has lapsed Login.gov MUST find the sign-in from its own records rather than refuse the issuance (D42). |
| **FR-BIL-9** | Reports MUST never count a person twice for one agency in one month because they arrived both directly and by delegation, and MUST NOT treat a person already seen directly as new when they later arrive by delegation. The delegated-only share of an agency's billed users MUST be reported alongside the totals. |
| **FR-BIL-10** | Onboarding MUST warn when an agency API's billing identity is not tied to a partner agreement, since delegated use of it would be recorded but never invoiced, and the delegation outcomes report MUST show that status per API. |

## Protocol rows

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **BIL-1** | Each successful exchange writes an `sp_return_logs` row for the agency with `issuer` the API's `billing_issuer`, `ial` derived as for direct access (2 when the person is verified and the service provider asserted IAL2 or IALmax, never the raw stored value), `returned_at` the exchange time, and the profile columns as the direct writer sets them. The billing issuer must be a real service provider issuer wired into the agency's agreement for the row to be invoiced. |
| **BIL-2** | `billable` is true for the first exchange per approval per `billing_issuer` per IAL and false afterwards. Refreshes write nothing. |
| **BIL-3** | The billable row's `request_id` is deterministic, `tx:<delegation_id>:<billing issuer>:<ial>`, so the existing unique index deduplicates; on a collision the row is retried with a random `request_id` and `billable: false` so the non-billable trail is kept. Session flags are not used, since the exchange runs outside the browser session. |
| **BIL-4** | `sp_return_logs` gains one column only: `access_type` (`direct` default, `delegated`; nullable, `sensitive=false`). No `actor_issuer`, `resource_server_identifier`, `delegated_proofing` or issuance-record reference is added to the return log; the link to the issuance record is a `delegated_token_issued` row in `sp_return_log_billing_adjustments`, which reports join through (D40). |
| **BIL-5** | The direct and delegated row writers share one service so they cannot drift. |
| **BIL-6** | Every consumer of `sp_return_logs` is reviewed for delegated rows: `UniqueMonthlyAuthCountsByIaa`, `TotalMonthlyAuthCounts`, `TotalMonthlyAuthCountsWithinIaaWindow`, `NewUniqueMonthlyUserCountsByPartner`, `Reports::DailyAuthsReport` and `Reporting::IdentityVerificationOutcomesReport`. Totals continue to include delegated rows; reports add an `access_type` breakdown; any column naming the service provider, the API or in-session proofing joins the issuance record and its approval through the adjustment row. |
| **BIL-7** | The approval records allow reporting, per service provider, agency, API and month: requested; withdrawn before use; consented and never exchanged (`first_exchanged_at` null and the authorization ended); consented and exchanged. `proofed_in_session` is recorded at consent, in the browser, where the identity-verification session is available (D68). |
| **BIL-8** | `profiles.initiating_service_provider_issuer` is not changed for delegation. When `proofed_in_session` is true for the approval, every agency that received a delegated access token in that sign-in is billed for it, proofing attribution included (the partner report counts the person for each such agency's partner), and the service provider's sign-in is waived (BIL-13). The attribution is a reporting rule in the invoice supplement, read from the joined approval, not runtime logic (D43). |
| **BIL-9** | The delegated billing row is written inside a savepoint so a unique-index collision on `request_id` does not abort the exchange's enclosing transaction; the collision is retried as the non-billable trail row (BIL-3). |
| **BIL-10** | Report filters on delegated rows use `COALESCE(access_type, 'direct')`, since pre-existing rows are NULL. The outputs of `TotalMonthlyAuthCounts`, `TotalMonthlyAuthCountsWithinIaaWindow` and `UniqueMonthlyAuthCountsByIaa` keep their row shapes (delegated rows included; direct plus delegated for one person bills once); the access-type breakdown on `DailyAuthsReport` is a separate `results_by_access_type` array. |
| **BIL-11** | `NewUniqueMonthlyUserCountsByPartner` keys a person by (user, profile, profile age, upfront) only; how the person arrived is not part of the key, so a person with a direct and a delegated row under one partner in a month is one user event and a person seen directly in one month is not new again by delegation later. The delegated facts are tallied alongside as `partner_ial2_unique_user_events_delegated_only` and `partner_ial2_unique_user_events_delegated_proofing`, the latter read from the joined approval's `proofed_in_session`. `UniqueMonthlyAuthCountsByIaa` reports `delegated_only_unique_users` per IAL and month. All three are trailing columns on the invoice supplement; each is a subset of a count to its left, never an addition. |
| **BIL-12** | In the delegation outcomes report, "authorization ended" means revoked, `remember_until` passed, or a single-authorization approval whose `rails_session_id` no longer matches the identity's; approved, current, unexchanged approvals count only in `requested`; there is no `declined` column, `withdrawn_before_use` (revoked by the person with reason `user_revoked` before any exchange) takes its place, `consented_not_exchanged` counts the other ended-unexchanged cases and `proofed_in_session_not_exchanged` counts approvals with in-session proofing and no exchange (D68). The report runs monthly over the calendar month, is saved to S3 as `<env>/delegation-outcomes-report/<year>/<YYYY-MM>.delegation-outcomes-report.csv` and is emailed to `delegation_outcomes_report_emails` (default `[]`). |
| **BIL-13** | `sp_return_logs` rows are never updated. At the handoff of a sign-in to a service provider approved for delegation, Login.gov writes a Redis entry `delegated-access-sign-in-return:<digest of the service provider's access token>` (`DelegatedAccess::OpaqueToken.digest`, the function the token store keys live entries with) holding the id of the sign-in's billable return-log row, TTL one hour (`token_exchange_billing_waiver_cache_seconds`, default 3600); the billable row's id is kept in the user session so a repeat handoff in the same session points the link at the invoiced row (D41). At the first exchange of a sign-in, in the transaction that writes the agency's billable row, Login.gov resolves the entry by the digest of the subject token and appends an `sp_return_log_billing_adjustments` row (`sp_return_log_id` the sign-in row, `delegated_return_log_id` the agency row, `token_exchange_token_id`, `adjustment_type` integer enum `exclude_from_billing` 1 / `delegated_token_issued` 2, `resolved_via` integer enum `cache` 1 / `database_fallback` 2). Invoice queries exclude a sign-in row that has an `exclude_from_billing` adjustment with `NOT EXISTS`; the waived count per service provider and month comes from the adjustments table. |
| **BIL-14** | Onboarding warns when an API's billing issuer is not an `integrations.issuer` (no partner agreement), through `TokenExchangeResourceServer#warn_if_unbillable`, called by `ServiceProviderSeeder` and `ServiceProviderUpdater`; the delegation outcomes report shows per API whether the billing issuer has an agreement. |
| **BIL-15** | On a cache miss the exchange falls back to the database: the most recent billable `direct` return-log row for the same user and the subject token's service provider issuer with `returned_at >= identities.last_authenticated_at`, and writes the adjustment from it with `resolved_via: database_fallback`. The exchange is never refused or delayed for billing; a miss with no row found is the `not_found` outcome of the `delegated_billing_waiver` analytics event (`outcome`, `already_waived`, `service_provider_issuer`, `billing_issuer`), which is the alert. The fallback can over-bill the service provider (row not found) or under-bill it (wrong row matched when the person signed in to the service provider more than once in the window); the match rule is under review by the data team and stands as built until it answers (D42, D75). |
| **BIL-16** | Later exchanges of the same sign-in find an existing adjustment for the sign-in row and write nothing; refreshes write nothing. The subject token's sign-in must still be live at the first exchange, and the fallback covers an exchange made after the hour. |

## As built

1. Migrations `20261009130000_add_access_type_to_sp_return_logs` (`access_type` string, nullable, default `direct`, `sensitive=false`; every report filter reads `COALESCE(access_type, 'direct')`, so the table is not rewritten) and `20261009130100_create_sp_return_log_billing_adjustments` (append-only; `SpReturnLogBillingAdjustment#readonly?` once persisted; `adjustment_type`, `resolved_via`, references to the sign-in row, the delegated row and the issuance record; indexes on (`sp_return_log_id`, `adjustment_type`), `delegated_return_log_id`, `token_exchange_token_id`).
2. `Billing::SpReturnLogWriter.write(user:, issuer:, ial:, request_id:, billable:, access_type:, retry_on_collision:)` is the only place an `sp_return_logs` row is created; `BillableEventTrackable#create_sp_return_log` calls it with the direct row's columns unchanged; the insert runs in a savepoint and `retry_on_collision: true` retries a billable collision once as a non-billable trail row.
3. `Billing::SignInWaiverLink` (`KEY_PREFIX = 'delegated-access-sign-in-return:'`, `digest` via `DelegatedAccess::OpaqueToken.digest`) writes the link at the handoff (`BillableEventTrackable#link_sign_in_for_delegated_billing`) for a service provider approved for delegation, TTL `token_exchange_billing_waiver_cache_seconds`; the token is never stored; a failure to write the link never affects the handoff.
4. `Billing::DelegatedReturnRecorder#call` runs at the end of `OpenidConnectTokenExchangeForm#issue!`'s transaction, in its own savepoint, every error reported and swallowed: the agency's row under `resource_server.billing_issuer_value` with `access_type: 'delegated'`, `ial` from `IalContext#bill_for_ial_1_or_2`, the profile columns as a direct row, `request_id` `tx:<delegation_id>:<billing issuer>:<ial>`; the `delegated_token_issued` adjustment linking the row to the issuance record; for a billable row the `exclude_from_billing` adjustment on the sign-in row found by cache or database fallback, or no row and the service provider stays billed. Outcome logged as `delegated_billing_waiver(outcome: cache_hit | db_fallback | not_found, already_waived, service_provider_issuer, billing_issuer)`.
5. Invoice queries: `UniqueMonthlyAuthCountsByIaa`, `TotalMonthlyAuthCountsWithinIaaWindow` and `NewUniqueMonthlyUserCountsByPartner` exclude adjusted sign-in rows with `NOT EXISTS` (`SpReturnLogBillingAdjustment.not_excluded_sql`); `delegated_only_unique_users` per IAL and month; the two partner columns with `SpReturnLogBillingAdjustment.proofed_in_session_sql` beside an unchanged per-user key, a delegated row whose approval records in-session proofing classified upfront for the agency's partner; `CombinedInvoiceSupplementReportV2` adds `iaa_ial2_delegated_only_unique_users`, `partner_ial2_unique_user_events_delegated_only` and `partner_ial2_unique_user_events_delegated_proofing` as trailing columns; `TotalMonthlyAuthCounts` unchanged; `DailyAuthsReport#results_by_access_type` beside its unchanged `results`; `Reporting::IdentityVerificationOutcomesReport` leaves delegated rows out of an agency's IAL2 proofing data.
6. `Reports::DelegationOutcomesReport` (monthly, `config/initializers/job_configurations.rb`) writes two CSVs: per service provider issuer, agency and API over the month's approvals (approvals replaced by a re-approval left out) `billing_issuer_has_agreement`, `requested`, `withdrawn_before_use`, `consented_not_exchanged`, `exchanged`, `proofed_in_session_not_exchanged`; per service provider the sign-ins still billed and waived, from the adjustments table. Saved under `delegation-outcomes-report` and `delegation-sign-ins-report` in S3 and emailed to `delegation_outcomes_report_emails` when set.
7. `ServiceProviderSeeder` and `ServiceProviderUpdater` call `TokenExchangeResourceServer#warn_if_unbillable` (`#billing_issuer_has_agreement?`) after writing an API record.
8. Configuration: `token_exchange_billing_waiver_cache_seconds` (integer, 3600) and `delegation_outcomes_report_emails` (json, `[]`) in `lib/identity_config.rb` and `config/application.yml.default`. Analytics: `delegated_billing_waiver` in `app/services/analytics_events.rb`. Migrations: the two above. Locale keys: none.
9. Not carried from earlier designs: `sp_return_logs.identity_id`, a `delegating_sign_in` access type, in-place waiving of a return log, and the exchange form's `bill_target` and `billing_request_id`; the dedupe key is the approval and the row carries the agency's billing issuer.
10. Specs: `spec/services/billing/{sp_return_log_writer,sign_in_waiver_link,delegated_return_recorder}_spec.rb`, `spec/models/sp_return_log_billing_adjustment_spec.rb`, `spec/controllers/concerns/billable_event_trackable_spec.rb`, `spec/services/db/monthly_sp_auth_count/*_spec.rb`, `spec/jobs/reports/{delegation_outcomes_report,combined_invoice_supplement_report_v2,daily_auths_report}_spec.rb`, `spec/lib/reporting/identity_verification_outcomes_report_spec.rb`, `spec/requests/openid_connect/token_exchange_spec.rb`, the seeder and updater specs; verified with the integrated stack; rubocop and the analytics-events lint clean.

## Decisions

- **D40** One marker column on the billing row; service provider, API and in-session proofing by join through the issuance record (full entry in section 16).
- **D41** The sign-in waiver link lives one hour, not the session lifetime.
- **D42** A cache miss at exchange falls back to the database and never blocks the exchange.
- **D43** Every agency that received a token in a proofed sign-in is billed for the verification; the service provider's sign-in is waived.
- **D68** The outcomes report counts withdrawals before use in place of declines.
- **D75** The items flagged for the data team stand as built with the current defaults until it answers.

## How to review

- Diff against `delegated-access-operations`. Billing changes follow the repository skill `.claude/skills/login-billing`; where a document disagrees with its checklist, the checklist wins.
- Check first: `Billing::SpReturnLogWriter` is called by the direct path with exactly its former columns (the direct row must not change); `sp_return_logs` is append-only and no code path updates a return log; the recorder's savepoint and error swallowing so billing can never fail an exchange; the request id shape; the `NOT EXISTS` in the three invoice queries and that `TotalMonthlyAuthCounts` is untouched; `SpReturnLogBillingAdjustment#readonly?`; `access_type` is the only new return-log column.
- Specs that prove it: the three `Billing::*` specs, the adjustment model spec, the trackable concern spec, the three invoice query specs, the outcomes report spec, the supplement and daily report specs, the identity-verification outcomes report spec, the exchange request spec.
- Must not change for existing clients: rows written before the column read as direct; a service provider not approved for delegation never writes a waiver link; invoice totals for a partner with no delegated rows are identical; `TotalMonthlyAuthCounts` output is unchanged.
- Known open items for the data team (D75; their review is still needed and nothing changes ahead of their answer): (1) the cache-miss fallback matching rule (user, service provider issuer, `returned_at >= identities.last_authenticated_at`) can match the wrong row for a person who signed in to the service provider more than once in the window and finds none for a row written before the identity was re-linked; the data team's original recommendation was no adjustment on a miss, so the decision to fall back needs their review, and `resolved_via` makes fallback use measurable; (2) proofing attribution mechanics: confirm how the invoice supplement classifies a delegated row as upfront for each agency through `token_exchange_grants.proofed_in_session` reached from the `delegated_token_issued` adjustment when `profile_requested_issuer` is the service provider, and how `access_type` keeps the service provider's agreement clear of that charge; (3) a lapsed agreement for an agency API: delegated rows are recorded and not invoiced, flagged by `billing_issuer_has_agreement`; confirm the report flag is sufficient; (4) whether the service provider pays anything for delegated use beyond its unwaived sign-ins, and who onboards its partner agreement; (5) recipients of the delegation outcomes report (`delegation_outcomes_report_emails`), and whether agencies and the service provider see it.
- Also handed over as built: a `not_found` outcome leaves the service provider billed for a sign-in an agency was also billed for, counted by `delegated_billing_waiver`; `TotalMonthlyAuthCounts` and `DailyAuthsReport` keep counting waived sign-ins, so they do not reconcile with the invoice queries for a delegating service provider; the supplement's three delegated columns are subsets of counts to their left and must not be added to them; the `access_type` backfill is implicit (`NULL` reads as `direct`) and a downstream extract reading the column raw must apply the same `COALESCE`.
- Held until the harness run: a durable identities-to-return-log link replacing the Redis waiver link (a billing change under the `login-billing` skill).

# 12. `delegated-access-fraud-signals`: fraud-signal delivery to agencies through the Attempts API

**Stack position** 12 of 14, on `delegated-access-billing-reporting`. **Historical plan section** 5.7.

## Objective

An agency that accepts delegated access on a person's behalf never saw the person sign in, so it would otherwise receive none of the fraud signals it gets when the person signs in to it directly. This branch gives the agency the same picture: while a delegation request is in flight, the sign-in session's Attempts API events (sign-in, multi-factor, rate-limit, identity-verification) are buffered in the KMS-encrypted part of the session, whether or not the service provider is itself enrolled; when the person approves an application, the buffered events are released to that application's enrolled agency under the person's identifier at that agency, together with a consent event and the stored identity-verification history, and later events of the browser session are forwarded live. Token issuance, renewal and revocation are reported to the agency as they happen, every delivered event carries the delegation identifier the agency's verification also returns, and the acting service provider is named, so the agency can join signals to API calls.

Nothing is delivered to, and no identifier is created at, an agency the person declined; a remembered approval reused without the consent screen still releases that sign-in's events with a consent event marked remembered. The gate for each recipient is Attempts API enrollment (listed as a provider with a usable encryption key); an agency that is not enrolled receives nothing and needs no change, and existing integrations receive their existing events unchanged. Delivery is on by default (D74) because enrollment is the agency's deliberate act and already the gate for its direct events; the privacy-review questions remain open and are that review's agenda. Server-side events written for the agency carry no network information, and the person's Login.gov session appears only as the same opaque hash the sign-in's events carry.

## Functional requirements

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **FR-FRD-1** | When a service provider requests delegation, Login.gov MUST retain the sign-in session's fraud-signal events (sign-in, multi-factor, rate-limit, identity-verification) for possible delivery, whether or not the service provider itself receives such events. |
| **FR-FRD-2** | When the person approves an agency, Login.gov MUST deliver those events to that agency through the existing Attempts API, attributed to the person's identifier at that agency, together with a consent event and the person's identity-verification history (once per agency, as today). |
| **FR-FRD-3** | Nothing MUST be delivered to, and no record of the person MUST be created at, an agency the person declined. |
| **FR-FRD-4** | While the person's sign-in to the service provider remains active, later events (re-authentication, multi-factor change, sign-out, timeout, rate limits) MUST also be delivered to each approved agency. |
| **FR-FRD-5** | Login.gov MUST deliver an event to the agency when a delegated token is issued, when it is renewed, and when access is revoked (with the reason). |
| **FR-FRD-6** | Every delivered event MUST carry the delegation identifier so the agency can join events to API calls, and MUST identify the acting service provider. |
| **FR-FRD-7** | When a remembered approval is reused without showing the consent screen, the agency MUST still receive the events for that sign-in and a consent event marked as remembered. |
| **FR-FRD-8** | Existing Attempts API integrations MUST continue to work unchanged; the new events are additive. Delivery of the new events is on by default and reaches only an agency enrolled in the Attempts API (listed as a provider, with a usable encryption key); an agency that is not enrolled receives nothing and needs no change. The privacy-review questions stay open and are that review's agenda (D74). |
| **FR-FRD-9** | Retained events MUST be encrypted while held and MUST be discarded when the session ends. |

Rows owned by other sections and reported to the agency by this branch: **FR-CEN-13** (every approval revocation is reported with its reason; section 7) and **FR-TOK-12** (reuse of a retired renewal credential is reported as `refresh_token_reuse`; section 7).

## Protocol rows

| Id | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **ATT-1** | When a delegation-enabled service provider's authorization request carries `token_exchange:*` values, Login.gov captures the sign-in session's Attempts events (sign-in, MFA, rate-limit, `idv-*`) into a buffer in the user session that the session encryptor protects, regardless of whether the service provider is enrolled in the Attempts API. The buffer holds at most 200 events (the earliest, the sign-in itself, are kept) and lives only as long as the session. Capture, release and fan-out run when `token_exchange_attempts_delivery_enabled` (default `true`), `token_exchange_enabled` and `attempts_api_enabled` are all on; the per-agency gate is enrollment (ATT-17), not the switch (D74). |
| **ATT-2** | At consent, for each approved application whose recipient is enrolled, Login.gov (a) creates the person's `AgencyIdentity` for that agency if missing (never an `identities` row); (b) delivers the buffered events re-mapped for that agency; (c) delivers one `delegated-access-consented` event per approval with `application`, `scope`, `remembered`, `source` (`consent_screen` or `account_page`) and `consented_at` (epoch seconds); (d) releases the historical `idv-*` events to that agency under the existing once-per-recipient rule. |
